Stripe Agentic Commerce Agent Services (Preview) 

Last modified: April 20, 2026

1. Stripe Agentic Commerce Agent Services.

These terms ("Agentic Commerce Agent Terms") supplement the General Terms, and if applicable to User, the Stripe Radar Terms, and govern User's use of the Agentic Commerce Agent Services. Capitalized terms not defined in these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms have the meanings given to them in the General Terms, the Stripe Radar Terms, the Stripe Connect Infrastructure Terms, the Stripe Payments Terms and the Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms.

2. User Obligations.

2.1 Documentation. User must integrate the Stripe API and use the Agentic Commerce Agent Services, including Stripe Elements, in accordance with the Documentation, this Agreement and any technical specifications that Stripe provides to User or publishes.

2.2 Stripe Elements. User must use Stripe Elements to facilitate Agentic Transactions.

2.3 Compliance. User must use the Agentic Commerce Agent Services in accordance with Law, Payment Method Requirements (including those relating to User's collection of consents from Agentic Customers and its provision of disclosures to Agentic Customers under Section 4.1 of these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms) and any obligations owed to Agentic Sellers.

2.4 Cooperation. User must reasonably cooperate with Stripe and any Agentic Seller, including to provide all necessary information and evidence to resolve Agentic Disputes, Agentic Refunds, and Agentic Customers' product liability claims, including transaction logs and relevant Agentic Customer Information.

2.5 PCI Compliance. User must, and must ensure that User’s service providers that store, access, or transmit Agentic Payment Account Details, comply with the PCI Standards. Upon Stripe’s request, User must provide to Stripe evidence of User’s compliance.

2.6 Payment Method Requirements. User must ensure that its use of the Agentic Commerce Agent Services complies with all applicable Payment Method Requirements, including requirements related to Agentic Payment Method presentment, use of Agentic Payment Method Marks, and the privacy, protection, collection, use, storage, and disclosure of data. Stripe may amend these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms, or introduce additional terms applicable to User, to reflect requirements imposed on Stripe or User by an Agentic Payment Method Provider. Stripe will provide User with reasonable prior written notice of any amendment under this Section, except where the Agentic Payment Method Provider requires immediate implementation, in which case Stripe will notify User as soon as reasonably practicable. Where User’s use of the Agentic Commerce Agent Services requires a waiver from an Agentic Payment Method Provider from any applicable Payment Method Requirements, User must obtain that waiver, ensure that it remains effective during the Term, and provide evidence of it to Stripe upon request. User will provide such information, cooperation, and assistance as an Agentic Payment Method Provider may reasonably request to enable and operate the Agentic Commerce Agent Services. User will respond to any request under this Section promptly and in any event within the timeframe specified by Stripe or the Agentic Payment Method Provider. An Agentic Payment Method Provider may enforce the terms of these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms directly against User with respect to that Agentic Payment Method Provider's Agentic Payment Method(s).

2.7 Data Quality. As required by the Payment Method Requirements and to the extent applicable to User's performance under these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms, User must: (a) meet data quality requirements for relevant categories of Agentic Transaction data (as described in the Payment Method Requirements); (b) comply with all technical specifications mandated by the relevant Agentic Payment Method Provider with respect to Agentic Transaction data; and (c) ensure that Agentic Payment Account Details, and any other information that User collects in connection with the Agentic Commerce Agent Services, is accurate and complete.

2.8 Risk Standards for Agents. Stripe may develop a framework for identifying trusted agents. To qualify as a trusted agent, User must comply with the criteria for trusted agents that Stripe provides to User or publishes.

3. Services Restrictions.

3.1 No Model Training. User must not use Agentic Catalog Data, Personal Data, Stripe Radar Data, Agentic Payment Account Details, or Agentic Transaction data (including aggregated data) to create, improve, train, or enhance User Systems or any third party's machine learning or artificial intelligence models, tools, technology, systems, modules, or algorithms.

3.2 No Profiling. User must not process Personal Data for the purpose of evaluating, analyzing, or predicting the characteristics or behavior of data subjects ("Profiling"). This includes the creation of behavioral profiles, predictive models, or "look-alike" audiences. User must not subject data subjects to any automated decision-making or Profiling that produces legal or similarly significant effects.

3.3 Agentic Catalog Data Use Restriction. User must not use Agentic Catalog Data received via Stripe for any purpose other than in connection with User's use of the Agentic Commerce Agent Services in accordance with these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms.

3.4 Stripe Radar Data. Stripe may provide User and Agentic Sellers (if applicable) Stripe Radar Data for the purpose of managing transaction fraud on Agentic Transactions. User must use any Stripe Radar Data: (a) solely for the purpose stated in this Section 3.4; and (b) in accordance with the Stripe Radar Terms. If there is a conflict between the terms of these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms and the Stripe Radar Terms, the Stripe Radar Terms prevail.

4. Disclosures, Information-Sharing and User Agreements.

4.1 Consents. User, and not Stripe, must obtain valid and verifiable consents from, and provide necessary disclosures and notices to, Agentic Customers, as required by Law and applicable Payment Method Requirements. These consents must authorize Stripe to lawfully use, store, and share Personal Data and Agentic Payment Account Details with Agentic Sellers and Stripe Connect Platforms (as applicable) to: (a) complete Agentic Transactions; (b) save and reuse Agentic Payment Account Details for future transactions; (c) provide services to Agentic Sellers; and (d) facilitate other data sharing or activities necessary to comply with these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms.

4.2 Compliance Records. User must: (a) retain auditable records of all Agentic Customer consents obtained, and disclosures provided under Section 4.1 of these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms; (b) make all necessary updates to its User Agreement (including its Privacy Policy) that may be required for the purposes of these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms, and (c) retain auditable records of User’s Authority at the time of each Agentic Transaction.  At Stripe's written request, User must promptly provide Stripe with documentation and evidence to verify User's compliance with this Section 4.2.

4.3 Agent Transactions Reports. User must provide to the Agentic Seller usage reports, performance data, and other relevant information about that Agentic Seller's Agentic Transactions where required by the Documentation or User’s agreements with Agentic Sellers or Agentic Customers. 

4.4 User Agreement with Agentic Customers. User must have a User Agreement to govern Agentic Transactions. The User Agreement must explain User’s Authority and include, at a minimum, an acknowledgement that: (a) if applicable, User may display the Agentic Catalog Data on the Agentic Interface; (b) Agentic Transactions are subject to the Agentic Seller's terms and conditions and Privacy Policy; (c) User may share Agentic Payment Details and Agentic Transaction Data with other parties in order to process Agentic Transactions, including with Agentic Sellers, Stripe, and, as applicable, Stripe Connect Platforms and Agentic Sellers’ other service providers; (d) an Agentic Seller is the seller of record for all Agentic Transactions; (e) an Agentic Seller is responsible for all order fulfillment and post-fulfillment obligations, including shipping, processing returns, issuing refunds, and facilitating exchanges; and (f) an Agentic Seller is solely responsible for Agentic Customers' claims relating to Agentic Seller's goods and services, and Agentic Customers must raise claims, disputes or questions about the goods or services directly with the Agentic Seller (and not against Stripe). User remains solely responsible for ensuring its User Agreement and Agentic Interface disclosures fully and accurately reflect the roles and responsibilities of all parties involved in an Agentic Transaction and comply with these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms, Laws, and Payment Method Requirements.

5. Stripe's Rights.

5.1 Transaction Submission. Agentic Sellers may use (a) the Stripe Payments Services to process Agentic Transactions, or (b) the Stripe Orchestrated Services to route Agentic Transaction data off of Stripe’s platform so that the Agentic Transaction may be processed by another payment services provider. Where the Agentic Seller uses the Stripe Payments Services, completion of the Agentic Transaction by Stripe is subject to the Agentic Seller, at its discretion, submitting the transaction and directing Stripe to process the payment. In either case, Stripe is not responsible for ensuring that an Agentic Seller submits a transaction for payment processing.

5.2 Transaction Refusal. Stripe and its Affiliates may decline, cancel, or refuse to facilitate any Agentic Transaction that Stripe reasonably believes is unauthorized, fraudulent, illegal, or exposes Stripe, User, Agentic Sellers, Agentic Payment Methods or Agentic Customers to unacceptable risk. Stripe is not liable to User for this refusal.

5.3 Seller Onboarding. Stripe is not liable to User, nor will Stripe be in breach of this Agreement, if Stripe or any Affiliate refuses to onboard or discontinues the provision of Stripe services or Stripe Technology to any Agentic Seller. 

5.4 Agentic Tokens. Agentic Payment Method Providers may request that Stripe provision Agentic Tokens for use in initiating Agentic Transactions, and User authorizes Stripe to: (a) provision Agentic Tokens on User's behalf, which may involve submitting information (including Agentic Customer Information and Agentic Seller information) to the Agentic Payment Method Provider; (b) share Agentic Tokens with Agentic Sellers, Agentic Payment Method Providers, and if applicable Stripe Connect Platforms; and (c) otherwise use those Agentic Tokens as reasonably necessary to facilitate Agentic Transactions.

6. Agentic Catalog Data.

6.1 User Obligations. If User receives Agentic Catalog Data, User must: (a) ensure that Agentic Catalog Data is displayed accurately, completely, in a timely manner, and does not violate Law; and (b) ensure that it has all necessary rights, consents, and authorizations from Agentic Sellers or third parties to receive and display the Agentic Catalog Data in the Agentic Interface.

6.2 License to Agentic Catalog Data. Stripe grants to User a non-exclusive, non-transferable, non-sublicensable, royalty-free, revocable license to display Agentic Catalog Data provided via Stripe to User on behalf of any Agentic Seller solely as necessary to perform User's obligations under these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms. 

6.3 Stripe Disclaimer. Agentic Catalog Data is provided by Agentic Sellers or third parties acting on the Agentic Seller's behalf. Stripe Parties disclaim all liability with respect to any claim relating to the accuracy, quality, currentness, or completeness of the Agentic Catalog Data or other order-related data for Agentic Transactions, unless a material defect in the accuracy, quality, currentness, or completeness of the Agentic Catalog Data is caused by Stripe.

7. License to User Systems. 

Subject to these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms, User grants to Stripe and its Affiliates a worldwide, non-exclusive, non-transferable (except in connection with a permitted assignment), non-sublicensable, royalty-free license during the Term to access and use any aspects of the User Systems necessary to enable the Services, as long as Stripe's access and use is: (a) solely as necessary to make the Services and related Stripe Technology available to User and Agentic Sellers under these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms; and (b) in compliance with these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms.

8. Liability for Use of Agentic Commerce Agent Services.

8.1 Agentic Interface, User Systems and User’s Authority. User remains fully responsible for, and Stripe Parties disclaim all liability with respect to any claim made by any person or entity, including Agentic Sellers and Agentic Customers relating to the Agentic Interface, User Systems (including any output or bias) or User’s Authority.

8.2 Disputes and Refunds. Stripe is not responsible for, and Stripe Parties disclaim all liability with respect to, Agentic Disputes, Agentic Refunds, or other Agentic Transaction-related Losses. 

8.3 Unauthorized Transactions. User will manage transaction fraud on Agentic Transactions on the Agentic Interface.  As between Stripe and User, User is responsible for unauthorized Agentic Transactions, if: (a) User fails to procure appropriate consents from the relevant Agentic Customer in accordance with these Agentic Commerce Agent Terms; (b) the Agentic Transaction was not authorized by the Agentic Customer, exceeded User’s Authority, or was caused by bugs, hallucinations or misinterpretations; or (c) the Agentic Interface, User Systems, or any AI agent operating within or on behalf of the Agentic Interface or User Systems, generates or initiates a transaction without an Agentic Customer manifesting, or User receiving, the Agentic Customer's intent to purchase whether due to error, malfunction, unauthorized action, or the agent acting without requisite authority or outside the scope of its permitted authority (each an "Unauthorized Agentic Transaction”).

8.4 Indemnification by User. User will defend the Stripe Parties against any Claim and indemnify the Stripe Parties against all Losses arising out of or relating to: (a) an Unauthorized Agentic Transaction; or (b) any allegation that User had unauthorized access to a third party's computer system or violated any third party's terms of service.

9. Definitions.

“Authority” means the express scope of authority an Agentic Customer grants to any AI agent operating within the Agentic Interface to initiate Agentic Transactions on Agentic Customer's behalf including any applicable spending limits, merchant restrictions, or time-bound parameters.

"Agentic Catalog Data" means information about an Agentic Seller’s goods or services, including but not limited to descriptions, disclaimers, links to terms or policies, product variants, images, pricing, availability and any additional data used to facilitate a sale to Agentic Customers.

"Agentic Commerce Agent Services" means the Services, including the provision of APIs and related infrastructure, that enable the Agentic Interface, as described in the Documentation.

"Agentic Customer" means an entity or individual, whether acting for itself or through an AI agent, who provides Authority or initiates a request to purchase goods or services via an Agentic Interface.

"Agentic Customer Information" means any information related to an Agentic Customer, including Agentic Payment Account Details, shipping information (name, email address, phone number, and address of the recipient), billing information (name, email address, phone number, and address of the Agentic Customer), and any information related to any restrictions, conditions, or limitations the Agentic Customer has placed on User’s Authority to act on behalf of the Agentic Customer.

"Agentic Disputes" means an instruction an Agentic Customer initiates to reverse or invalidate a processed Agentic Transaction (including "chargebacks" and "disputes" as those terms may be used by Agentic Payment Method Providers).

"Agentic Interface" means User's agentic interface that enables Agentic Customers to make an Agentic Transaction. 

"Agentic Payment Account Details" means the Agentic Payment Method account details for an Agentic Customer that PCI Standards require to be protected, which may include the Agentic Customer's name, and with respect to credit and debit cards, the Agentic Customer's account number, card expiration date, and card verification value or similar security code.

"Agentic Payment Method" means any payment method available to Agentic Customers at the point of purchase in the Agentic Interface.

"Agentic Payment Method Provider" means the provider of an Agentic Payment Method (e.g., Visa Inc.).

"Agentic Refunds" means an instruction an Agentic Seller initiates to provide a full or partial return of funds to an Agentic Customer for a processed Agentic Transaction.

"Agentic Seller" means a third party seller of goods or services that has made its goods or services available for  purchase by Agentic Customers.

"Agentic Token" means a payment credential issued by an Agentic Payment Method Provider corresponding to an Agentic Payment Method, specifically for use by an Agentic Seller to initiate Agentic Transactions via User, and any other data related to that payment credential (e.g., cryptograms).

"Agentic Transaction" means a transaction between an Agentic Customer and an Agentic Seller that is initiated by an Agentic Customer for the purchase of goods or services offered by an Agentic Seller.

“Payment Method Requirements” means: (a) the publicly available guidelines, bylaws, rules, and regulations an Agentic Payment Method Provider imposes that describe how an Agentic Payment Method may be accepted and used; and (b) terms that apply to the acceptance and use of an Agentic Payment Method, located on or accessible from the Stripe Website, including on the Stripe Legal Page, and which as of the Effective Date are described on that page as “Payment Method Terms”.

"PCI-DSS" means the Payment Card Industry Data Security Standards.

"PCI Standards" means PCI-DSS and Payment Card Industry Software Security Framework (PCI-SSF), including successor standards (if any).

“Stripe Elements” means Stripe's pre-built UI components for creating payment flows.

“User Agreement” means, as to each Agentic Customer, collectively, the agreements that User has with that Agentic Customer.

“User System” means any User software, hardware, system, artificial intelligence model, tool, technology or algorithm (a) used by User, or (b) made available by User in connection with the activities contemplated under these Agentic Commerce Agent Service Terms.

Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services (Preview)

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services.

These terms (“Agentic Commerce Seller Terms”) supplement the General Terms, the Stripe Financial Services Terms, and if applicable to User and its Integration, the Stripe Payments Terms, the Stripe Connect Terms, the Stripe Radar Terms, and the Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms, and govern User’s use of Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Agentic Commerce Seller Terms have the same meanings given to them in the Agreement.

2. Processing AC Transactions.

Stripe will make Stripe Payments Services available for AC Transactions submitted by User to Stripe. The Stripe Payments Terms apply to AC Transactions when User uses Stripe Payments Services to process AC Transactions. Certain Integrations require the use of Stripe Payments Services, as described in the Documentation. The Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms apply if User processes AC Transactions with third party payment services providers.

3. User Obligations.

3.1 Stripe Profile. To use the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services, User and each Participating Seller (if applicable and if a Connected Account) may be required to (a) create a Stripe profile that provides complete and accurate business information that Stripe reasonably requires to provide the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services; and (b) maintain that Stripe profile and keep all information provided current.

3.2 Order Fulfillment Responsibility. User (whether itself or through its Participating Sellers), and not Stripe, is responsible for (a) fulfilling AC Customer orders from AC Transactions; (b) providing AC Customer with an itemized receipt for the AC Transaction; and (c) addressing any customer-service-related issues for AC Transactions, including warranty claims, returns, exchanges, disputes, refunds, and reversals. 

3.3 Merchant of Record. User or Participating Seller (if applicable), and not Stripe or the AC Interface Provider, is the “merchant of record” for AC Transactions. Stripe and AC Interface Provider will not, at any time, take title to any goods sold via the AC Interface.

3.4 Fraud Mitigation. User and Participating Sellers (if applicable) will manage transaction fraud on AC Transactions. User may use Stripe Radar Data received from Stripe (if any) for that purpose in accordance with the Stripe Radar Terms. Any Radar Scores and other fraud signals shared with User relating to User, Participating Sellers (if applicable), or AC Transactions, constitute Stripe Radar Data. 

3.5 Use of Checkout and Shared Payment Tokens. By using the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services in connection with AC Transactions, User authorizes AC Interface Provider to (a) create a Shared Payment Token to facilitate User’s or its Participating Seller’s AC Transactions; and (b) for certain Integrations, initiate a checkout session on User’s behalf. User must not (x) access or use any payment data received via a Shared Payment Token except as necessary for post-transaction customer engagement; (y) attempt to use a Shared Payment Token for more than one authorization (except as expressly permitted by the Documentation); or (z) attempt to use Shared Payment Tokens that are expired or revoked. If required by the Documentation, User will report usage of the Shared Payment Token via the Payment Records API.

3.6 Acceptance of Payment Methods. Using Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services in connection with accepting or enabling the acceptance of a Payment Method constitutes User’s acceptance of the applicable Payment Method Rules and Payment Method Terms. Where User’s use of Agentic Commerce Seller Services or Orchestrated Services requires a Payment Method Provider’s waiver from any applicable requirements, User must obtain that waiver, ensure it remains effective, and provide evidence of it to Stripe upon request. Payment Method Rules and Payment Method Terms are Financial Provider Terms for purposes of this Agreement.

3.7 Platform Users and Participating Sellers. This section applies if User is a Stripe Connect Platform and any of User’s Connected Accounts are Participating Sellers. The Stripe Connect Terms apply to the Stripe Connect Platform and AC Transaction(s) are an “Activity”, under the Stripe Connect Terms. 

(a) User will designate Participating Sellers to applicable AC Interface Providers and Stripe, and enable Participating Sellers to make goods or services available for purchase to AC Customers via an AC Interface.

(b) Each Participating Seller that is a Connected Account is considered a “User” of the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services and the obligations of User under these Agentic Commerce Seller Terms also apply to the Connected Account. 

3.8 Terms of Service, Disclosures, and Consents. 

(a) If AC Interface Provider presents disclosures to AC Customers on behalf of User, User is responsible for reviewing mock ups of an AC Interface Provider’s proposed disclosures to obtain necessary consents from AC Customers and any mock ups of proposed checkout flows, to the extent mock ups are made available to User for review by the AC Interface Provider. 

(b) User or AC Interface Provider (depending on the Integration), and not Stripe, is responsible for either (i) ensuring User’s terms of service, privacy policy and disclosures are included as part of the Catalog Data, or (ii) ensuring that AC Customers are presented with and accept any required terms of service, privacy policy and disclosures in connection with an AC Transaction . 

(c) Stripe is not responsible for obtaining, and relies on AC Interface Providers to obtain, all necessary consents from AC Customers to enable Stripe to provide  the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services and facilitate AC Transactions. 

3.9 Indirect Taxes. User is solely responsible for calculating, collecting, and remitting Indirect Taxes on AC Transactions.

4. Catalog Data.

This Section 4 applies only to an Integration in respect of which User directly or indirectly provides Catalog Data to Stripe. An Integration that does not involve the provision of Catalog Data to Stripe, such as in connection with Link’s Agent Wallet, is not subject to this Section 4:

4.1 Catalog Data and Order-related Data.

User must ensure that Catalog Data and any other information that User provides to Stripe or the AC Interface Provider in connection with the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services is current, accurate, complete, not misleading, does not infringe or violate the rights of any third party, and complies with Law, including advertising and consumer protection Law. By using Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services to enable AC Transactions, User, and not Stripe, is responsible for obtaining current, accurate and complete data from AC Customers in connection with AC Transactions. 

4.2 Catalog Data License.  

(a) User must provide (directly or indirectly through a third party product catalog service provider) all necessary Catalog Data to Stripe. User must ensure that Stripe has all necessary rights to use, reproduce, display, retain, and share with the AC Interface Provider Catalog Data in connection with the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services.

(b) User grants to Stripe a worldwide, royalty-free, non-exclusive, sublicensable license to use, reproduce, display, retain, and share with the AC Interface Provider Catalog Data in connection with providing, personalizing, customizing, developing, and improving the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services and other related Services.  User acknowledges that this license is sublicensable to AC Interface Providers and their affiliates for the purposes of providing, personalizing, customizing, developing and improving their AC Interfaces and related services.

(c) Stripe will ensure that Catalog Data provided by Stripe to an AC Interface Provider on behalf of User is consistent with the Catalog Data received by Stripe from User. 

5. Limited License to Marks.

This Section 5 applies only where an Integration will involve Stripe identifying User (or a Participating Seller, if applicable) to Customers or AC Interface Providers in connection with the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services. Where an Integration does not involve Stripe identifying User in this way, such as in connection with Link’s Agent Wallet, this Section 5 will not apply:

User grants to Stripe (on behalf of itself and Participating Sellers, if applicable) a worldwide, non-exclusive, sublicensable, royalty-free license during the Term to use User’s and Participating Seller’s (if applicable) Marks within AC Interfaces and in connection with AC Transactions as necessary to provide the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services.

6. Additional Stripe Rights.

Stripe may, at its discretion, refuse to onboard User or any Participating Seller to Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services if Stripe believes User or Participating Seller presents an unacceptable risk to Stripe. Stripe may also, at its discretion, decline, cancel, refuse to facilitate or otherwise provide Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services in conjunction with any AC Transaction that Stripe believes (a) may violate these Agentic Commerce Seller Terms, the Agreement, or any other agreement between Stripe and User or a Participating Seller; (b) is unauthorized, fraudulent or illegal; (c) violates Law or Payment Method Rules; or (d) exposes, or is likely to expose, Stripe to unacceptable risk.

7. AC Interface Provider Rights.

User acknowledges that AC Interface Provider may, at its discretion, (a) choose whether to enable transactions between AC Customers and User or Participating Sellers (if applicable), (b) decline to feature or allow User or Participating Seller (if applicable) to sell on its AC Interface, (c) not find, surface, or recommend User’s and Participating Sellers’ (if applicable) goods or services, and (d) reject User’s Catalog Data, in whole or in part, if it violates AC Interface Provider’s policies. User acknowledges that completion of an AC Transaction is subject to the AC Interface’s enablement of the transaction. User acknowledges that AC Interface Provider controls the discoverability of User’s and Participating Sellers’ (if applicable) goods and services. When User elects to enable AC Transactions, User agrees to the applicable AC Interface Provider terms of service, which are made available to User either by Stripe or by AC Interface Provider.

8. Fees.

In addition to Fees listed on the Stripe Pricing Page or otherwise agreed in writing for Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services,  the Fees for any other Services used in relation to AC Transactions or as part of Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services will apply to User, including Fees for Stripe Payments Services and Stripe Radar Services. If directed by AC Interface Provider, Stripe may deduct fees payable by User to the AC Interface Provider from AC Transactions processed through the Stripe Payments Services and remit those fees to AC Interface Provider.

9. Stripe Disclaimers.

User remains fully responsible for, and Stripe Parties disclaim all liability with respect to (a) any claim, including product warranty, product liability, product defect, property damage, personal injury, or consumer protection claims, related to the goods or services sold as part of an AC Transaction; (b) any claim related to the accuracy, quality, currentness, or completeness of the Catalog Data or other order-related data (e.g. tax information) for AC Transactions.

10. Conflicts.

In the event of a conflict between these Agentic Commerce Seller Terms and the Stripe Payments Terms, Stripe Financial Services Terms, Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms, or Stripe Connect Terms, to the extent such terms apply to User and its Integration, the provisions of these Agentic Commerce Seller Terms will prevail for the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services. In the event of a conflict between these Agentic Commerce Seller Terms and the Stripe Radar Terms, the provisions of the Stripe Radar Terms will prevail.

11. Definitions.

“AC Customer” means an individual or entity, whether acting for itself or through an AI agent, who initiates a request to purchase goods or services from User or a Participating Seller (if applicable) via an AC Interface enabled by Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services. An “AC Customer” is a “Customer” under the Agreement and “Data Subject” under the Data Processing Agreement.

“AC Interface” means an agentic interface   that enables end users to initiate a request to purchase goods or services from third party sellers.

“AC Interface Provider” means the entity providing the AC Interface. 

“AC Transaction” means a transaction between an AC Customer and User or Participating Seller (if applicable), that is initiated by an AC Customer for the purchase of goods or services offered by the User or Participating Seller.

“Catalog Data" means information about a User’s or Participating Seller’s (if applicable) goods or services, including but not limited to descriptions, disclaimers, links to terms or policies, product variants, images, pricing, availability and any additional data used to facilitate a sale to AC Customers.

“Indirect Taxes” means any applicable transaction taxes and duties imposed by any Governmental Authority, including sales and use tax, value-added tax (VAT), and goods and services tax (GST), or equivalent transaction taxes.

“Integration” means the specific configuration, selection, and technical implementation of the Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services deployed by User, as described in the applicable Documentation.

“Participating Seller” means either (a) a merchant offering its goods and services to end customers through User’s platform and User is using Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services; or (b) a Connected Account designated by User, that is enabled to offer its goods or services to end customers using Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services.

“Shared Payment Token” means a limited reference to a payment method that is created by an AC Interface Provider and shared with User or Participating Sellers to facilitate AC Transactions. 

“Stripe Agentic Commerce Seller Services” means the Services, including Shared Payment Tokens and other Stripe Technology, as described in the Documentation, that enable User to offer goods or services to AC Customers and enable checkout and payment processing for AC Transactions.

Stripe Atlas Services

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Stripe Atlas Services.

These terms (“Stripe Atlas Terms”) supplement the General Terms and govern User’s use of the Stripe Atlas Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Atlas Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms. 

2. User Information.

User acknowledges that the Stripe Atlas Services rely on the accuracy and completeness of information User provides to Stripe, and User is solely responsible for the accuracy and completeness of all information. Inaccurate or incomplete information may: (a) result in Stripe not being able to provide some or all of the Stripe Atlas Services; and (b) cause the documents in connection with the Stripe Atlas Services to be inaccurate or incomplete.

3. Authorizations and Consents.

3.1 Doing Business Electronically. 

By using the Stripe Atlas Services, User consents to doing business electronically. 

3.2 Electronic Signature.

The Stripe Atlas Services include processing various legal documents for User. User acknowledges that it will be legally bound by these documents and authorizes Stripe to automatically apply User’s signature(s) electronically to all documents necessary to provide the Stripe Atlas Services. 

3.3 Tax Identification Number. 

If User uses  the Stripe Atlas Services to obtain a tax identification number from the U.S. Internal Revenue Service, User authorizes Stripe to use the User Information for such purpose..

3.4 Registered Agent. 

User authorizes Stripe to designate a Registered Agent. User must inform Stripe of any action related to its Registered Agent that it takes independently, including changes and dissolutions. 

3.5 AI Agent.

If User uses an AI Agent to access the Stripe Atlas Services, User is solely responsible for each action initiated by or through the AI Agent. User acknowledges that: (a) an AI Agent constitutes an "electronic agent" or equivalent concept as defined or recognized under the Uniform Electronic Transactions Act (UETA) and similar statutes; and (b) actions initiated or completed by an AI Agent are legally binding on User.

4 Fundraising. 

4.1 Compliance.

If User uses the Stripe Atlas Services for a Fundraising, User must: (a) comply with all Laws relating to a Fundraising; and (b) ensure that information and materials it provides to investors through the Stripe Atlas Services are accurate, complete, and not misleading.

4.2 User Responsibilities.

User is solely responsible for:

(a) determining whether, and on what basis, a Fundraising may be made, including the availability and satisfaction of any registration exemption, investor eligibility or verification requirements, required disclosures, notices, filings, and recordkeeping obligations.

(b) ensuring that each purchaser satisfies the eligibility requirements on which User relies for its Fundraising, including, if required, confirming that a purchaser is an “accredited investor” (within the meaning of Regulation D under the Securities Act of 1933). 

(c) the tax characterization, reporting, filing, withholding, and payment obligations of User that arise from any Fundraising. 

5. Providers.

Stripe may enable or allow User to access services provided to User by Providers. User authorizes: (a) Providers to deliver services and interact with Government Authorities as required to deliver the relevant services, and (b) Stripe to share data necessary to deliver the same. User acknowledges that its access to and use of Registered Agent services, and automated state and federal filing processing is subject to the LegalZoom Provider Terms.

6. Acknowledgments, Disclaimer and Liability. 

6.1 Acknowledgments.

User acknowledges and agrees that:

Stripe Billing

Last modified: June 17, 2026

1. Stripe Billing Services.

These terms (“Stripe Billing Terms”) supplement the General Terms and govern the use of the Stripe Billing Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Billing Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms.

2. Third-Party Services; LLM Services and Fees.

2.1 Certain features of the Stripe Billing Services allow User to access and use Third-Party Services. LLM Services are Third-Party Services. Stripe may collect fees (including LLM Fees) from User for User’s use of Third-Party Services via the Stripe Billing Services. User’s obligation to pay fees and Stripe’s rights to collect fees under this Agreement apply to those fees.

2.2 User may integrate directly with an LLM Provider or may use another Third-Party Service through which to integrate with an LLM Provider. If User integrates directly with a Third-Party Service provider (including an LLM Provider), or uses another Third-Party Service through which to integrate with an LLM Provider, User is solely responsible for and must pay directly to the LLM Provider or Third-Party Service provider (as applicable) any fees for use of the Third-Party Services.

2.3 User may be required to create an account with a Third-Party Service provider to use Third-Party Services.

3. User’s Obligations.

3.1 User is responsible for configuring the Stripe Billing Services for its needs and to comply with Law. 

3.2 User must (a) ensure the accuracy of information User provides to Stripe in connection with the Stripe Billing Services, and (b) resolve any disputes between User and Customers related to User’s use of the Stripe Billing Services.

3.3 To access Third-Party Services (including LLM Services) via the Stripe Billing Services, User must activate those services by selecting the relevant Third-Party Service provider (e.g., LLM Provider) in its Stripe Dashboard or by integrating via the Stripe API. 

3.4 If User uses the Services to submit recurring or subscription Transactions, then before submitting the initial Transaction, User must (a) inform each Customer that the relevant Transactions will occur on an ongoing basis; and (b) explain the method for canceling the Customer’s recurring billing or subscription.

3.5 If User accesses Third-Party Services (including LLM Services) via the Stripe Billing Services, User must provide notices to, and obtain rights and consents from, Customers sufficient to permit Stripe to lawfully receive, handle, transmit and process Customer Personal Data, including as contained in LLM Data between User and an LLM Provider.

4. Services Restrictions.

4.1 User must not use the credits feature of the Stripe Billing Services to (a) sell gift cards; (b) offer stored value to User’s customers; (c) offer a wallet functionality; or (d) enable the purchase of products or services from third parties. 

4.2 User must not use the Stripe Billing Services in ways expressly prohibited in the Documentation. 

4.3 User must not, and must ensure that its Connected Accounts (if applicable) do not, use the Stripe Billing Services in connection with Protected Health Information.

4.4 User must not use LLM Services in violation of the LLM Services’ terms of service and policies.

4.5 User must not use the Stripe Billing Services (a) for activities that are subject to International Traffic in Arms Regulations (ITAR) maintained by the United States Department of State; or (b) in a manner that breaches, or causes the breach of Law.

4.6 Stripe reserves the right, at its sole discretion, to suspend, restrict, or remove User's access to any Third-Party Service (including LLM Services) available via the Stripe Billing Services, with or without prior notice and without liability to User. 

5. Indemnification. 

Subject to the Limitations on Indemnity in the General Terms, User will indemnify Stripe, its Affiliates, and their directors, employees, and agents for all Losses arising from User’s material breach of an LLM Provider’s terms of service and policies. 

6. Definitions.

“Connected Account” means (a) a Platform User that has a Stripe Account onboarded to a Stripe Connect Platform via the Stripe Connect Services; or (b) a Payout Recipient.

“LLM” means large language model.

“LLM Data” means any data User directs Stripe to send to or receive from an LLM Provider in connection with User’s use of the Stripe Billing Services.

“LLM Fees” shall mean the fees charged by the LLM Provider to Stripe at the time of query and response for User’s use of the LLM Services. 

“LLM Provider” means a third-party provider of an LLM that User selects and enables for use via the Stripe Billing Services.

“LLM Services” means an LLM and related services provided by an LLM Provider, which User accesses via the Stripe Billing Services.

“Stripe Billing Services” means the Services that enable User to bill Customers for products and services, collect revenue, and manage subscriptions, including the Stripe Metronome Services.

“Stripe Metronome Services” means the Services that are Metronome-branded, including the Services that enable User to meter usage events, configure usage-based and flexible pricing models, and manage contracts and commitments for Customers based on usage or other consumption metrics.

Stripe Capital for Platforms

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Stripe Capital for Platforms.

These terms (“Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms”) supplement the General Terms and, together with the Stripe Financial Services Terms and Stripe Connect Infrastructure Terms each of which are incorporated into these Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms by this reference, govern the use of the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms, the Stripe Financial Services Terms, or the Stripe Connect Infrastructure Terms. The Stripe Capital for Platforms Services enable User’s Connected Accounts to obtain Stripe Capital Financing in conjunction with User’s Platform Services.

2. Stripe Capital Financing.

2.1 Nature and Use of Stripe Capital Financing.

(a) Use of Stripe Capital Financing. User must ensure that Prospects only apply for, and Financing Recipients only use, Stripe Capital Financing in compliance with all Laws and only for their Business Purposes (and not for personal, family or household use). User must immediately notify Stripe if, at any time, User becomes aware that a Financing Recipient has used or is using the proceeds of a Stripe Capital Financing for any purpose other than Financing Recipient’s Business Purpose (and not for personal, family or household use) or in any manner that does not comply with all Laws. User must maintain all regulatory licenses, registrations, authorizations, permits, and other permissions (or waivers) required by any Governmental Authority in connection with its activities and obligations under these terms. User must undertake all marketing financing-related activities in connection with the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services and these Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms only in the Approved Territory.

(b) Nature of Stripe Capital Advance. A Stripe Capital Advance is not intended to be, nor will it be construed as, a loan or any other lending transaction.

2.2 Stripe as a Capital Provider.

User acknowledges that Stripe is a Capital Provider only in certain Approved Territories in which Stripe offers Stripe Capital Advances, and only with respect to Stripe Capital Advances.

2.3 Marketing to Prospects.

(a) Identifying Prospects. Upon Stripe’s request, User must cooperate with Stripe to identify Prospects for Stripe Capital Financing.

(b) Marketing Materials. User must comply with Stripe’s guidelines and directions when creating Marketing Materials. User must submit all Marketing Materials that User intends to use in connection with Stripe Capital Financing or the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services, including any Marketing Materials based on templates provided by Stripe, for approval by Stripe before they are used.

(c) Marketing to Connected Accounts. User is only permitted to market (i) Stripe Capital Financing to User’s Connected Accounts, and (ii) Prequalified Offers only to Prospects, in each instance within the Approved Territory. User must comply with all guidelines and instructions from Stripe, or Stripe on behalf of a Capital Provider, and the Documentation when marketing Stripe Capital Financing.

(d) Removal of Marketing Materials. User must immediately discontinue using any Marketing Materials if Stripe notifies User that (i) the Marketing Materials do not adhere to Stripe’s guidelines, Stripe’s instructions, or the Documentation; or (ii) the Marketing Materials present unacceptable risk to Stripe or a Capital Provider.

2.4 Prequalified Offers.

(a) User-Presented Prequalified Offer. If User has elected to present Prequalified Offers to Prospects, User must present all Prequalified Offers in the form and substance approved by Stripe. User must present a Prequalified Offer for a Prospect within 1 business day after User receives it. User may present a Prequalified Offer to a Prospect via email or by any other method approved by Stripe in writing. If User erroneously presents a Prospect or Connected Account with a Prequalified Offer, or if a Capital Provider decides to revoke a Prequalified Offer, then at User’s expense, User must immediately take corrective action as instructed by Stripe.

(b) Stripe-Presented Prequalified Offer. If Stripe erroneously presents a Prequalified Offer to a Prospect or Connected Account, or if a Capital Provider decides to revoke a Prequalified Offer, then at Stripe’s expense, User must immediately take corrective action as instructed by Stripe.

(c) Revoked Prequalified Offers and Cancelled Stripe Capital Financings. Stripe may, on behalf of itself or a Capital Provider, revoke a Prequalified Offer or cancel or terminate a Stripe Capital Financing for any reason whether before or after funds have been disbursed. Stripe disclaims all liability to User with respect to Stripe Capital Financings, including if a Prequalified Offer is revoked, or if a Stripe Capital Financing is cancelled or terminated.

(d) No Guarantee. User’s use of the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services does not in any way guarantee that a Connected Account will receive a Prequalified Offer.

2.5 Applying for Stripe Capital Financing.

(a) Applications for Stripe Capital Financing. User must ensure that all Prospects apply for Stripe Capital Financing via the Application Flow. If a Prospect contacts User, or User contacts a Prospect, regarding how to complete an application for Stripe Capital Financing, User must redirect that Prospect to the Application Flow via a process provided or approved by Stripe.

(b) No Selection Permitted. Neither User nor a Prospect may select or demand a specific Capital Provider or type of Stripe Capital Financing.

2.6 Financing Terms.

User must ensure that all Prospects that become Financing Recipients cooperate with Stripe to comply with its applicable Financing Terms and can access financing-related information.

2.7 Payment Processing Through Stripe.

Except as otherwise permitted by Stripe in writing, User must use the Stripe Payments Services for (a) all Financing Recipients with outstanding obligations under a Stripe Capital Financing until those obligations have been fully satisfied, and (b) all Prospects whose eligibility for Stripe Capital Financing is then-currently under review. User must not terminate or restrict a Financing Recipient’s access to User’s Platform Services or the Stripe Payment Services, except (x) if a Financing Recipient ceases to be a Platform User, or (y) in the event of credit risk, fraud, or other reasons which would pose an unreasonable legal or reputational risk to User. If User terminates or restricts the access of a Financing Recipient to User’s Platform Services and that Financing Recipient has outstanding obligations under a Stripe Capital Financing, then User must immediately notify Stripe, and take all actions necessary, as directed by Stripe, to ensure that the Financing Recipient pays those outstanding amounts in full.

2.8 Stripe API and Stripe Dashboard.

If Stripe makes the Stripe API or Stripe Dashboard available to User, then User may use them in accordance with the Documentation to access the Program Data and Marketing Materials and to provide Connected Accounts and Prospects with access to Stripe Capital Financing.

2.9 Additional Fees.

User must not charge User’s Connected Accounts additional fees in connection with Stripe Capital for Platforms Services or a Stripe Capital Financing.

2.10 Platform Access.

User must, at no cost to Stripe, provide Stripe with an account on User’s platform, and provide access to all surfaces on User’s platform that feature, reference, or embed components of Stripe Capital for Platforms Services to enable Stripe to use, test, and monitor User’s use and marketing of the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services and compliance with these Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms.

2.11 Communication with Platform Users, Connected Accounts, Prospects, and Financing Recipients.

(a) General. Unless otherwise described in this Section 2.11, User must use Pre-Approved Responses when responding to questions or other communications from Platform Users, Connected Accounts, Prospects, or Financing Recipients about the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services or Stripe Capital Financing. If a Platform User, Connected Account, Prospect, or Financing Recipient needs assistance that a Pre-Approved Response cannot resolve, then User must redirect that Platform User, Connected Account, Prospect, or Financing Recipient to Stripe or otherwise follow Stripe’s instructions for resolution. If User contacts a Platform, User, Connected Account, Prospect, or Financing Recipient for any other reason regarding the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services or Stripe Capital Financing, User must do so only in accordance with Stripe’s guidelines or instructions and the Documentation. Stripe will provide all Pre-Approved Responses and User must not modify a Pre-Approved Response in any way.

(b) Complaints. User must not use Pre-Approved Responses to respond to Complaints. User must direct Platform Users, Connected Accounts, Prospects, and Financing Recipients to submit Complaints regarding the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services and Stripe Capital Financing to capital-support@stripe.com within 3 business days after receipt. User may elect to reply to inquiries or feedback from User’s employees about the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services.

2.12 Training.

User must ensure that all employees, contractors, and agents with responsibilities related to Stripe Capital for Platforms Services receive training that complies with the guidelines that Stripe provides to User in writing (if any).

2.13 Outsourcing Prohibited.

Unless otherwise approved by Stripe in writing, User must not outsource or subcontract User’s obligations under these Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms to any third party.

2.14 Monitoring; Audit.

Stripe may monitor and review User’s Stripe Account, User’s use of the Stripe API, User’s marketing activities, and any other information, policies and procedures or agreements, consents, and disclosures that User makes available to User’s Platform Users, to ensure User’s compliance with these Stripe Capital for Platforms Terms. 3. Servicing.

If applicable, the Servicer will provide all Servicing to Financing Recipients.

4. Confidentiality; Data Privacy and Security.

4.1 Data Protection.

Each party must be compliant with all Laws governing the privacy, protection, security, confidentiality, and the use of data provided, accessed or used in connection with the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services. User must:

(a) (i) obtain all necessary consents, and authorizations, and record and manage those consents and authorizations and (ii) provide disclosures, including from and to User’s Platform Users, in each case to disclose Platform User Data to Stripe and enable Stripe to collect, use, process, or disclose Personal Data and other information User provides to Stripe or a Capital Provider in connection with the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services or a Stripe Capital Financing, including consents and authorizations for marketing and underwriting purposes; and

(b) ensure that Platform User Data does not include a Platform User's end users' “personal data” as defined in the GDPR or “personal information” as defined in the CCPA.

User must not be a consumer credit reporting governmental agency or entity in an Approved Territory, and User must ensure that its data sharing under these Capital for Platforms Service Terms will not subject Stripe to any consumer credit reporting Laws in an Approved Territory.

User must not disclose Program Data to any third parties except as necessary to process Transactions for Financing Recipients with Stripe, and in accordance with Law and Card Network Rules, or as otherwise directed by Stripe.

4.3 Stripe’s Use of Data.

Stripe processes, analyzes, and manages Data in accordance with Stripe’s Privacy Policy to (a) determine eligibility to become a Prospect, (b) provide the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services, (c) provide Servicing, (d) mitigate fraud, financial loss, or other harm, (e) fulfill Stripe’s obligations to regulatory authorities, Capital Providers, credit facilities involved in the financing of loans, and investors who may purchase loans, and (f) analyze, develop, and improve Stripe’s products, systems, and tools. Stripe provides Data to third-party service providers, Capital Providers, credit facilities involved in the financing of loans, investors who may purchase loans, and Stripe’s Affiliates, to allow Stripe to provide the Stripe Capital for Platforms Services to User, Platform Users, Connected Accounts, and other users. User consents to Stripe’s use of Data for the purposes and in a manner consistent with this Section 4.3. Stripe may retain Platform User Data it receives from User in accordance with applicable Law and Stripe’s retention policies.

Stripe Climate

Last modified: November 18, 2025

1. Stripe Climate.

These terms (“Stripe Climate Terms”) supplement the General Terms, and govern User's use of Stripe Climate Commitment and Stripe Climate Orders. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Climate Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms. Stripe, LLC provides Stripe Climate Commitment, and for the purposes of Stripe Climate Commitment, each reference to “Stripe” in these Stripe Climate Terms and in the General Terms will be read as a reference to Stripe, LLC. Frontier Climate Operations, LLC (“Frontier”), Stripe’s Affiliate, provides Stripe Climate Orders, and for the purposes of Stripe Climate Orders, each reference to "Stripe" in the General Terms will be read as a reference to Frontier.

2. Stripe Climate Commitment.

2.1 Opting In and Out, and Changes.

If User elects to contribute Climate Commitment Funds, User must opt in to contributions via the Stripe Account onboarding process or the Stripe Dashboard. User may opt out of, change, or pause a contribution at any time in User's Stripe Dashboard.

2.2 Climate Commitment Funds.

(a) Climate Commitment Funds Allocation. Stripe or its Affiliate may deduct the Climate Commitment Funds from User's Stripe Account balance or User Bank Account on a per-Transaction basis, or as an amount aggregated over a period of time, as described in User's Stripe Dashboard. Stripe will allocate all Climate Commitment Funds to advance the field of carbon removal, through activities such as purchasing CRUs, or funding research and development for new carbon removal technologies. Climate Commitment Funds are not refundable.

(b) Stripe Climate Commitment Goals. The goal of Stripe Climate Commitment is to help advance the field of carbon removal, and Climate Commitment Funds will be used in support of that goal. While Stripe will use reasonable efforts to fund impactful Climate Projects, companies receiving Climate Commitment Funds are developing new technology in an evolving industry and their survival and success in addressing climate issues are not guaranteed. Development timelines of many new technologies are long, the resulting impact may not be immediate, and there is always a risk that the chosen technologies fail to deliver any carbon removal. Even if they are eventually successful in obtaining results, Climate Projects may take years to produce those results.

(c) Use of Climate Commitment Funds. Stripe will determine when to allocate Climate Commitment Funds to Climate Projects, and Stripe will inform User about how Climate Commitment Funds were allocated. Stripe will not apply Climate Commitment Funds toward its own corporate or administrative costs.

(d) No Carbon Credits. The carbon removal services Stripe purchases are not certified by any governmental or non-governmental body. They are not “authorized” under Article 6 of the Paris Agreement. Stripe disclaims all warranties regarding (i) the status under applicable governmental or non-governmental programs or pursuant to standards set by governmental or non-governmental bodies, whether implemented now or in the future, (ii) the availability of any current or future tax credit, benefit, or treatment, or (iii) any other financial benefits, associated with the carbon removal services that Stripe purchases.

(e) Taxes. The Climate Commitment Funds may not be eligible for a tax deduction. User should consult with its tax or legal advisor on the local tax treatment of Climate Commitment Funds. Climate Commitment Funds exclude all Taxes, except as the Documentation states to the contrary.

3. Stripe Climate Orders.

3.1 User's Use of Stripe Climate Orders.

User must use Stripe Climate Orders in compliance with these terms for Stripe Climate Orders. User must not use the Stripe Climate Orders for personal, family, or household use.

3.2 CRUs.

(a) Frontier uses Offtake Agreements to acquire CRUs. To the extent commercially reasonable, Frontier will review (i) the sources of CRUs; and (ii) the terms governing the generation of those CRUs.

(b) Frontier will make Retirement Services available to User for purchase subject to: (i) the availability of CRUs in carbon markets; (ii) Frontier’s ability to secure all necessary rights (including contractual rights) for the future delivery of the CRUs; and (iii) Frontier’s receipt of those CRUs. User may verify the availability of CRUs for Retirement, and purchase Retirement Services using the Climate API or the Stripe Dashboard. While Frontier will provide User with information about the CRUs available for Retirement, it is User's responsibility to verify that the CRUs meet User's needs or the needs of a Beneficiary.

(c) Frontier may be unable to acquire sufficient quantities of CRUs to fulfill orders for Retirement Services. The CRU(s) that Frontier ultimately Retires on User's behalf may have been generated from an Offtake Agreement that Frontier executed after User's initial purchase. In order to provide Retirement Services, Frontier reserves the right to: (i) substitute CRUs from any one carbon removal supplier with a CRU from a different carbon removal supplier; or (ii) allocate then-current inventory of CRUs among Purchasers.

(d) The CRUs are not “authorized” under Article 6 of the Paris Agreement. Frontier disclaims all warranties regarding (i) the status of CRUs under applicable governmental or non-governmental programs or pursuant to standards set by governmental or non-governmental bodies, whether implemented now or in the future, (ii) the availability of any current or future tax credit, benefit, or treatment, or (iii) any other financial benefits, associated with User's purchase of Retirement Services or the associated CRU(s).

3.3 Retirement.

(a) On or before the last day of the Retirement Year, Frontier: (i) on User's behalf, will Retire the CRU; or (ii) if applicable, will transfer title to the CRU to User. If User has designated a Beneficiary, Frontier will Retire the CRU on that Beneficiary’s behalf and include the Beneficiary’s name in the Registry, if permitted. Frontier will Retire and record the CRU in the applicable Registry in accordance with and subject to the rules of that Registry.

(b) Frontier may not be able to, or may not be permitted to, Retire and record partial CRUs (i.e., partial tons) with a Registry. Frontier may combine partial CRUs and record them in Frontier’s name.

(c) If Frontier Retires a CRU on User's behalf or on behalf of a Beneficiary, Frontier will notify User of the Retirement via the Climate API, the Stripe Dashboard, or, if applicable, by other means User elects in the Stripe Dashboard, and deliver a certificate to User evidencing Retirement. Certificates will be digital.

(d) In the event Frontier does not receive sufficient CRUs to fulfill all orders for Retirement Services, Frontier reserves the right, in its sole discretion, to: (i) allocate CRUs among Purchasers; or (ii) refund the Fees.

(e) If Frontier determines that it is unable to Retire a CRU in User's name or in the Beneficiary’s name on or prior to the last day of the Retirement Year of the CRU, Frontier will notify User and, in its sole discretion, Frontier may: (i) refund the applicable Fees; (ii) transfer title to the CRU to User; (iii) record the Retirement of the CRU in Frontier’s name and provide certification to User that Frontier completed the Retirement in Frontier’s name; or (iv) take other reasonable remedial measures. Neither Frontier nor Stripe will pay User any interest on Fees, and Frontier will have no further obligation to User regarding that Retirement and the associated CRU(s) nor any other obligation to complete any additional Retirements. Frontier will not be liable to User for any other losses, damages, or costs that User or others may suffer arising out of or relating to Frontier’s inability to perform the Retirement Services or the associated CRU.

(f) If Frontier is unable to contact User, or is unable to provide a refund of Fees, for any reason (e.g., User has closed its Stripe Account), Frontier will contribute any amounts due to User to carbon removal programs of Frontier’s choosing.

(g) Frontier will use commercially reasonable efforts to ensure a CRU can be Retired on User's behalf or on behalf of User's designated Beneficiary. Frontier does not guarantee that Frontier will be able to Retire a CRU on behalf of a Beneficiary.

3.4 Beneficiaries.

User may designate a Beneficiary when User purchases Retirement Services. User is responsible for notifying a Beneficiary of a CRU’s Retirement. Frontier will not notify a Beneficiary of a CRU’s Retirement. These Stripe Climate Terms are intended solely for the benefit of the parties to these Stripe Climate Terms. Frontier has no obligation to any Beneficiary under these Stripe Climate Terms. When User designates a Beneficiary, User will not provide Frontier with a Beneficiary’s Personal Data.

3.5 Power of Attorney.

To the extent necessary, User grants to Frontier a power of attorney to act on User's behalf or on behalf of a Beneficiary to provide Stripe Climate Orders. User represents as of the Effective Date and warrants during the Term that User has secured the necessary rights and consents from the applicable Beneficiaries to enable Frontier to act on behalf of the applicable Beneficiaries.

3.6 Suspension and Termination.

Frontier may, at any time or for any reason, suspend or terminate User's access to Stripe Climate Orders, including User's ability to advertise or publicize any CRUs or Stripe Climate Orders.

3.7 Disclaimer.

User agrees that Frontier offers solely the Retirement of CRUs, and that Frontier is not acting as a marketplace, dealer, or seller of carbon removal units or offtakes. Other than as provided herein, the Stripe Parties (including Frontier) make no representation or warranty of any kind whatsoever for Stripe Climate Orders or a CRU, including the availability of a CRU at any given time or the validity of a CRU’s generation.

4. Term.

These Stripe Climate Terms will apply to all Retirement Services purchased prior to termination and the associated CRUs until (a) Frontier Retires the relevant CRUs on User's behalf or User's Beneficiary’s behalf, (b) the relevant CRUs have been transferred to User, or (c) Frontier refunds some or all of the Fees for the Retirement Services in the manner described in Section 3.3 of these Terms.

5. Definitions.

“Beneficiary” means a business entity (i.e., not a natural person) that is an intended beneficiary of a Retirement or Retirement Services.

“Carbon Removal Unit,” or “CRU,” means a specified amount of carbon dioxide removed from the atmosphere that is acquired via Offtake Agreements. CRUs may be either a full or a partial ton.

“Climate API” means the APIs that facilitate User’s selection of Retirement Services and any associated CRUs, and the sample code, instructions, requirements, and other guidelines as described in the Documentation.

“Climate Commitment Funds” means the amount User chooses to voluntarily allocate to Climate Projects through Stripe Climate Commitment, as a percentage of User’s revenue or a flat monthly amount, or another method of calculation Stripe accepts.

“Climate Project” means a climate project that Stripe funds.

“Frontier Marketing Collateral” means the marketing and promotional materials and other ancillary documents developed for the purpose of promoting Stripe Climate Orders.

“Offtake Agreement” means a legally binding contract to buy future tons of carbon dioxide removal at an agreed price if and when delivered.

“Paris Agreement” means the Paris Agreement to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change, U.N. Doc. FCCC/CP/2015/L.9/Rev/1 (Dec. 12, 2015).

“Purchaser” means a user that purchases Retirement Services.

“Registry” means any carbon offset registry or system that tracks the Retirement of a CRU.

“Retire” or “Retirement” means the permanent removal of a specified CRU from all applicable carbon markets. Retirement may be further governed by the rules of a Registry.

“Retirement Services” means all actions taken to Retire a CRU in the selected Retirement Year.

“Retirement Year” means the year User selects, either via the Climate API or in the Stripe Dashboard, for a CRU to be Retired.

“Stripe Climate Commitment” means a suite of features Stripe provides that are designed to enable User to create and run User’s own corporate climate program.

“Stripe Climate Orders” means (a) the Retirement Services offered to User; (b) access to the Climate API and the Climate Orders portion of the Stripe Dashboard; (c) access to the Frontier Marketing Collateral; and (d) all other services provided by Frontier and its Affiliates to facilitate the purchase or the provision of, as applicable, the foregoing, including the integration with the payment processing services provided by Stripe.
