Stripe Managed Payments

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Stripe Managed Payments Service.

1.1 Operation of these terms.

These terms (“Stripe Managed Payments Terms”) supplement the General Terms, the Stripe Financial Services Terms, the Stripe Payments Terms, the Stripe Connect Terms, each of which are incorporated into these Stripe Managed Payments Terms by this reference, and, together with the Documentation, govern User’s use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service. If the Stripe Managed Payments Terms conflict with the Financial Services Terms, the Stripe Payments Terms, or the Stripe Connect Terms, then the Stripe Managed Payments Terms control as applied to the Stripe Managed Payments Service. The Stripe Managed Payments Service is provided by Stripe’s Affiliate, Sold through Link, LLC (“SMP”). These Stripe Managed Payments Terms create an agreement between User and SMP for use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service. User agrees that SMP may freely assign and transfer any of its rights or obligations under these Stripe Managed Payments Terms to its Affiliates. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Managed Payments Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms, the Stripe Financial Services Terms, the Stripe Payments Terms, or the Stripe Connect Terms.

1.2 Description of Stripe Managed Payments Service.

The Stripe Managed Payments Service enables the sales of digital products to Customers by surfacing checkout pages to Customers, sending receipts, managing sales proceeds and post-sale support (e.g., disputes and transaction support), as well as calculating and remitting any applicable Indirect Taxes in certain jurisdictions, as described in the Documentation.

2. Use of Stripe Managed Payments Service.

2.1 Provision of Services by SMP to Seller. 

(a) Merchant of Record. SMP provides the Stripe Managed Payments Service to User, as a Seller, and not to User’s Customers. SMP is the merchant of record for Transactions using the Stripe Managed Payments Service. User appoints and authorizes SMP to facilitate the sale of User’s Product on User’s behalf in accordance with these Stripe Managed Payments Terms, and User agrees to be bound by the actions of SMP in connection with the sale of User’s Product on User’s behalf. User agrees that SMP is not the seller of record, SMP does not have or receive possession of the Product, and SMP will not book the revenue from User’s sale of the Product to Customer. User acknowledges and agrees that, for Indirect Tax purposes only and as further described in Section 4 (Responsibility for Indirect Taxes on the Sales of Products), SMP should be treated under applicable Laws as making or deemed to make the supply of the Product to the Customer, as an electronic interface operator, marketplace operator, commissionaire, agent or deemed supplier, or as the person responsible for determining, collecting, reporting and remitting Indirect Taxes in respect of that supply.

(b) Changes to User's Stripe Account. As part of providing the Stripe Managed Payments Service to User, SMP and its Affiliates may need to make changes to User’s Stripe Account, on User’s behalf, (i) enable a payment method or adjust its settings for use with the Stripe Managed Payments Service, (ii) submit evidence in connection with a Dispute for a Stripe Managed Payments Service Transaction, (iii) enable or configure fraud prevention and dispute management tools (e.g., dispute deflection and automated resolution) to maintain acceptable risk and dispute levels, (iv) delete, restrict access to, or modify Customer information or Transaction data, as requested by a Customer or required by Law, in connection with a Stripe Managed Payments Service Transaction. User authorizes SMP and its Affiliates to make the changes described in this Section.

2.2 Provision of Services by Stripe to SMP; Pooled Accounts.

SMP may provide access to the Stripe Payment Services in order to facilitate User’s sale of Products to Customers. User appoints SMP as User’s agent for the limited purpose of directing, receiving, holding and settling funds under this Agreement. All settlement funds that Stripe, SMP, or Acquiring Affiliates, as applicable, receive for Transactions are combined with settlement funds for other users and held in one or more Pooled Accounts at one or more Financial Providers. Once an Acquiring Affiliate receives funds for a Transaction, the relevant Customer has no further obligation to make payments to User with respect to that Transaction, regardless of whether and when Stripe or its Affiliate (as applicable) settles those funds to User. If Stripe or SMP (as applicable) does not settle funds due to User under this Agreement, User will have recourse only against Stripe or SMP (as applicable) and not the relevant Customer. Stripe or SMP (as applicable) will promptly update User’s Stripe Account balance to reflect processed Transactions. If, in the capacity as User’s agent, SMP provides a receipt for a Transaction to the applicable Customer, that receipt is binding on User. Stripe and its Affiliates (as applicable) may invest funds held in a Pooled Account as described in the General Terms. User has no rights to any Pooled Account, or earnings generated by funds held in any Pooled Account, and are not entitled to draw funds from any Pooled Account.

2.3 Provision of Services by Stripe to Customers. 

Stripe and its Affiliates may separately provide other Stripe services to Customers. 

3. User's Use of the Service.

3.1 Use of the Service and Termination. 

Stripe Managed Payments Service only applies to, and may only be used for, Transactions for eligible Products that User elects to sell via the Stripe Managed Payments Service. By using the Stripe Managed Payments Service, User’s Stripe Account is treated as a Connected Account to SMP as the Platform. These Stripe Managed Payments Terms incorporate by reference the Connected Account Agreement. SMP reserves the right to suspend or terminate User’s use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service at any time. If SMP or its Affiliates determines that User’s products do not qualify as eligible Products, SMP and its Affiliates may (i) require User to stop using the Stripe Managed Payments Service for the ineligible products, or (ii) offboard the ineligible products from the Stripe Managed Payments Service.

3.2  Subscriptions.

For any Product sold on a subscription basis via the Stripe Managed Payments Service, User must clearly and conspicuously disclose the terms of the subscription to Customers before or at checkout, including subscription amount and frequency of the charge, term length, whether the subscription automatically renews, the subscription amount upon renewal, any additional fees and costs related to the subscription, and a method for cancellation. If User elects to manage its existing Customer subscriptions for Products through Stripe Managed Payments Service, User will, as of the date it starts using the Stripe Managed Payments Service and at all times during the Term: 

(a) have the necessary contractual rights to assign or transfer existing Customer subscriptions to be managed through Stripe Managed Payments Service and to appoint SMP to charge and collect subscription amounts owed by Customers;

(b) have appointed SMP as User’s agent before SMP charges and collects subscription amounts on User’s behalf from User’s Customers, and will maintain that agency appointment; and

(c) will give notice to User’s Customers that SMP will be responsible for charging and collecting subscription amounts owed by Customers. 

If User elects to stop managing Customer subscriptions for Products through Stripe Managed Payments Service to manage them directly, User will, as of the date of User’s election: 

(x) have the necessary contractual rights and capabilities to manage the subscriptions and to charge and collect subscription amounts owed by Customers; and

(y) will give timely notice to the affected Customers that SMP will no longer charge and collect subscription amounts owed for the sale of User’s Product, that there is no further relationship between SMP and User’s Customers, and any services provided by Stripe or its Affiliates to Customers related to those subscriptions will no longer be provided.

3.3 Refund Policy.

SMP reserves the right to issue refunds within 60 days of a Customer’s purchase of a Product via the Stripe Managed Payments Service regardless of any more restrictive refund policy User has set with its Customers. User’s refund policy remains subject to the SMP refund policy, located at https://support.link.com/questions/sold-through-link-refunds, at all times. In the event of a conflict between the SMP refund policy and User’s refund policy, the SMP refund policy will control where User’s refund policy is more restrictive than the SMP refund policy. If SMP is required to make a Refund by Law, including Law applicable to Customer's location, User is liable for the full amount of the Refund regardless of any refund policy. User, as Seller, is liable to SMP and its Affiliates for the full amount of any Disputes, refunds, and Reversals resulting from User’s sale of Products using the Stripe Managed Payments Service. SMP or its Affiliates will debit the amount of any Disputes, refunds, and Reversals from User’s Stripe Account balance or, where permitted by Law, User’s User Bank Account.

3.4 Customer Disputes and Refunds. 

(a) User Cooperation. User will have an opportunity to address Customer complaints and Disputes. If SMP contacts User for additional information about the sale of a Product, User has an opportunity to respond within 48 hours of the request and provide to SMP the requested information. If User does not provide the requested information or a timely response, SMP may refund the payment to the Customer without User’s input. 

(b) Dispute Rate Management. SMP may, at its discretion, enable and configure dispute prevention, deflection, and resolution features on User’s behalf to manage User’s dispute rate and maintain the integrity of the Stripe Managed Payments Service. User is responsible for any fees associated with dispute management tools, including when enabled on User’s behalf, as described in the relevant Documentation or on the Stripe Pricing Page.

3.5 Seller Information.

User authorizes Stripe and its Affiliates to provide User Information to Stripe’s Financial Providers in connection with User’s use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service. 

3.6 Payment Methods.

The Stripe Payments Terms apply when User uses the Stripe Managed Payments Service. Section 3 (“Payment Methods and Transactions; Payment Method Rules and Payment Method Terms”) of the Stripe Payments Terms are modified to apply to User as the Seller when using Stripe Managed Payments Service and any reference to Stripe Payments Services is replaced with the Stripe Managed Payments Service. For clarity, when using the Stripe Managed Payments Service, (a) references to submitting Transactions refers to SMP submitting Transactions on User’s behalf, and (b) Section 3.5 of the Stripe Payments Terms (“American Express Conversion – Only Applicable to the American Express Card Network”) does not apply. User, as Seller, is responsible for compliance with the specific requirements for acceptance and use of Payment Methods that are offered to Customers through User’s use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service, unless already complied with on User’s behalf by the Stripe Managed Payments Service (e.g., providing a receipt that accurately describes each Transaction).

3.7 Liability for Payment Methods.

(a) Pass-Throughs. Stripe will pass through to User and User is responsible for (a) any costs, fees, fines, and charges imposed by a Payment Method Acquirer, Payment Method Provider, or Card Network related to User’s sale of Products via the Stripe Managed Payments Service, and (b) the liability allocated to SMP (acting on User’s behalf as the Seller) from a decision made by a Payment Method Acquirer, Payment Method Provider, or Card Network related to User’s sale of Products via the Stripe Managed Payments Service. Any decision made by a Payment Method Acquirer, Payment Method Provider, or Card Network is final and binding on User. 

(b) Stored Payment Methods. User may have access to Payment Method information used by a Customer for Transactions using Stripe Managed Payments. User must not charge a Customer’s stored Payment Method if the original authorization was obtained by SMP, unless User obtains its own authorization from Customer in accordance with Law.

3.8 Transaction Integrity.

SMP may decline, cancel, or refund any Transaction made using Stripe Managed Payments Service at any time if SMP reasonably believes or suspects a Transaction (a) may be fraudulent or unlawful, (b) poses a security risk to Stripe or its Affiliates, (c) may be abnormal (e.g., excessive order size), (d) may be made in error, (e) may be made in bad faith, (g) is likely to result in a Dispute, or (h) is a violation of this Agreement, the Documentation, or other Stripe terms or policies.

4. Responsibility for Indirect Taxes on the Sales of Products.

4.1 Indirect Taxes Responsibility.

(a) SMP Tax Responsibility. By using the Stripe Managed Payments Service, User agrees SMP will calculate, collect, hold, and remit to the relevant Government Authorities Indirect Taxes on the sale of Seller’s Product in the countries identified in the Documentation. For Sellers located in certain jurisdictions, SMP will only calculate, collect, and remit Indirect Taxes on the cross-border sales of Seller’s Product (i.e. for sales to Customers outside of Seller’s country), and SMP will have no responsibility for Indirect Taxes on the domestic sales of Seller’s Product (i.e., for sales to Buyers in Seller’s own country).  As applicable under local Law, User appoints SMP to act as an electronic interface operator, marketplace operator, commissionaire, or agent on User’s behalf to facilitate the supply of User’s Product to Customers for Indirect Tax purposes. User acknowledges that, in jurisdictions where SMP, as an electronic interface operator, marketplace operator, commissionaire, or agent, is treated by Law as making the supply in SMP’s own name or as an intermediary, SMP will be treated as the supplier or intermediary liable for Indirect Taxes, and will be responsible for charging, collecting, reporting and remitting those Indirect Taxes to the relevant Governmental Authority.

(b) Seller Tax Responsibility and Customer Invoices. For Sellers located in jurisdictions where SMP only supports Indirect Taxes for cross-border sales of Seller’s Product, Seller is solely responsible for calculating, collecting, holding, and remitting Indirect Taxes to the relevant Government Authority on domestic sales of Seller’s Product. For Sellers with Customers located in jurisdictions not identified in the Documentation, SMP is not responsible for the calculation, collection, or remittance of Indirect Taxes on the sale of Seller’s Product, and Seller is solely responsible for the calculation, collection, and remittance of all taxes as required by Law. For those sales of Products using Managed Payments for which SMP is not responsible for the Indirect Taxes, SMP will issue an invoice to Customer on behalf of Seller in Seller’s name. Seller, and not SMP or its Affiliates, is responsible for ensuring that invoices issued by SMP in Seller’s name meet Seller’s legal and tax requirements. Seller should configure the invoice settings in the Stripe Dashboard and ensure the invoices comply with Law, including determining if the invoices need to be supplemented using additional third party services to meet Seller’s legal and tax requirements.

(c) Responsibility for Indirect Tax Refunded to Customers. User must pay SMP for any Indirect Tax that is refunded to Customers in connection with a Transaction using Stripe Managed Payments Service, if SMP or its Affiliates is required to remit the Indirect Tax amount to Governmental Authorities for that Transaction and is unable to recover the relevant Indirect Tax amounts from the Governmental Authority. User authorizes Stripe and its Affiliates to debit User’s Stripe Account balance and recover from User any Indirect Taxes refunded by Stripe and its Affiliates to User’s Customers, where SMP is required to remit those Indirect Taxes to, and is unable to recover those Indirect Taxes from, the relevant Governmental Authority. If Stripe or its Affiliates can recover those  Indirect Taxes from the relevant Governmental Authority at a later date, Stripe or its Affiliates will refund the recovered  amount to User.

(d) Tax Inclusive. In any jurisdiction where, User is deemed to make a supply of the Product to SMP and SMP is deemed to make a supply of the Product to User’s Customer:

(i) the amounts payable by SMP to the User for the Product are inclusive of any Indirect Taxes that apply to the deemed supply of the Product to SMP; and

(ii) User will not  charge or recover any additional amount from SMP for that Indirect Tax.

(e) Undisclosed Agent. User acknowledges that SMP will communicate with and invoice the customer in SMP’s own name, without disclosing that SMP is acting as User’s agent. If User does not want SMP to act as its agent, User must not use Stripe Managed Payments Service for those transactions.

(f) Free Trials Provided by User. If User offers Free Trials to User’s Customers and the Free Trial is deemed a taxable supply where SMP is treated as the party responsible for the Indirect Tax, User authorizes SMP to:

(i) determine, using SMP’s reasonable discretion and based on Law, whether Indirect Tax is due on User’s Free Trials;

(ii) charge, collect, withhold, and remit Indirect Tax in accordance with Law; and

(iii) charge or recover from the User the amount of any Indirect Tax attributable to  User’s Free Trials.

User will promptly reimburse SMP, upon request, for any Indirect Tax, interest, penalties, or associated costs that SMP has paid, is assessed, or becomes liable for in relation to User’s Free Trials. SMP may, without limitation, set off such amounts against any sums otherwise payable or due to the User under this Agreement or debit User’s Stripe Account balance. Upon request, User must  provide SMP with accurate and complete information regarding the nature, value, and terms of any Free Trials.

4.2 Product Eligibility and Tax Rates.

User must provide accurate information to SMP about its products to determine eligibility for the use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service. For each Product that User elects to sell via the Stripe Managed Payments Service the Stripe Managed Payments Service will determine the appropriate product taxability and tax rate. Stripe and its Affiliates reserve the right to review and change the tax rate assigned to a Product at its discretion for a Transaction using the Stripe Managed Payments Service that SMP supports for Indirect Tax purposes in accordance with the Documentation. User acknowledges and agrees that any change Stripe makes to a tax rate does not constitute tax advice. SMP is not responsible for the calculation, collection, or remittance of Indirect Taxes on the sale of any product that does not meet the eligibility requirements as described in the Documentation, and Seller is solely responsible for the calculation, collection, and remittance of all Taxes as required by Law.

4.3 Tax Authorities and Audits.

If User receives any correspondence from a Governmental Authority in connection with SMP’s Indirect Tax responsibilities as stated in Section 4.1 (Indirect Taxes Responsibility), User must (i) contact Stripe Support by the earlier of (a) five calendar days after receipt of that correspondence or (b) five business days before the response deadline. After receipt of notification, Stripe will endeavor to provide User with information or supporting documentation to respond to the Government Authority and User must reasonably assist Stripe in its review of the matter. Stripe may require User to request an extension from a Governmental Authority if the response deadline is less than five business days from Stripe’s receipt of that correspondence. In any case, User will respond to the Government Authority only after engaging in discussion with Stripe. Any delay or failure to notify will not relieve Stripe of its obligations under this Section 4.3, except to the extent Stripe has been prejudiced by the delay or failure.

4.4 Responsibility for other Taxes.

User is responsible for payment of other Tax liabilities that arise from User’s sale of a Product via the Stripe Managed Payments Service. If Stripe becomes liable for any other Tax liabilities as a result of User’s use of Stripe Managed Payments Service or these Stripe Managed Payments Terms, excluding Tax liabilities owed by Stripe or SMP as a result of charging Fees for the Stripe Managed Payments Service, User authorizes Stripe to recoup the cost of other Tax liabilities by deducting funds from amounts Stripe owes to User. 

For any Tax Information Reports, User acknowledges and agrees that User is in the best position to determine the nature of the payments made in connection with User’s use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service. With respect to US tax law, in connection with User’s use of the Stripe Managed Payments Service, Stripe and its Affiliates are not the withholding agent for Form 1042-S purposes.

4.5. Responsibility for Invoicing.

This Section 4.5 applies for Tax purposes only. User agrees to a self-billing arrangement for User’s deemed provision of the Product to SMP in the jurisdictions where such an arrangement is permitted by Law as outlined in the Documentation. Where permitted, User agrees to accept all self-billed invoices issued by SMP on User’s behalf and User must not issue its own invoices to SMP or its Affiliates for the deemed sales of Product to SMP. User remains responsible for determining whether self-billing is permitted in User’s jurisdiction. If self-billing is not permitted in User’s jurisdiction, User must inform SMP immediately by contacting Stripe support and may, if required by Law, issue compliant Tax invoices for all deemed sales of Product to SMP or its Affiliates.

User must review the self-billed invoices in User’s Stripe Dashboard and must notify SMP of any errors by contacting Stripe support.

User must provide SMP its Indirect Tax registration number (e.g., VAT/GST number). User must immediately inform SMP in writing if (a) User’s Indirect Tax registration number changes, (b) User ceases to be registered for Indirect Tax purposes in any jurisdiction, or (c) User’s Indirect Tax status is otherwise altered. Failure to do so may result in the invalidation of the self-billed invoices.

User is responsible for providing all necessary and accurate information for SMP to generate the self-billed invoices. User may opt out of this self-billing arrangement by notifying Stripe in writing. If User opts out, then User is responsible for providing SMP invoices for all deemed sales of Product made by User to SMP.

5. Taxes on Fees.

(a) Taxes. This Section 5 (Taxes on Fees) replaces section 7.3 (Taxes) of the General Terms. The Fees exclude all Taxes, except as the Stripe Pricing Page or other documents expressly state to the contrary. If SMP or its Affiliates are required by Law to collect or withhold any Taxes, SMP or its Affiliates will collect or withhold such Taxes as additional amounts to the Fees and either deduct those Taxes from amounts otherwise owed to User or charge User for the additional Taxes if there are insufficient amounts owed to User from which to deduct the Taxes, and pay those Taxes to the appropriate taxing authority. If User is exempt from paying, or is otherwise eligible to pay a reduced rate on, those Taxes, User will provide to SMP a copy of the original certificate that satisfies applicable legal requirements attesting to User’s tax-exempt status or reduced rate eligibility, in which case SMP or its Affiliates will not deduct the Taxes that certificate covers. Failure to provide SMP a valid VAT or GST identification number may result in the assessment and applicability of Taxes, which will be deducted from User’s Stripe Account balance or Stripe may invoice User for those amounts. 

(b) User Information. User must provide accurate information regarding User’s tax affairs as SMP or its Affiliates reasonably request, and must promptly notify SMP if any information that Stripe prepopulates is inaccurate or incomplete. SMP or its Affiliates may send documents to User and any relevant Government Authority for transactions processed using the Services. Specifically, Law may require SMP or its Affiliates to file periodic informational returns with a Government Authority related to User’s use of the Services. SMP or its Affiliates may send tax-related information to User electronically. User’s failure to provide accurate, complete, or up‑to‑date information or documentation when required by these Stripe Managed Payments Terms or the Documentation is a material breach of these Stripe Managed Payments Terms.

6.2 Customer Data Deletion.

In compliance with applicable DP Law, SMP will honor valid deletion requests from Customers who made a purchase through Stripe Managed Payments Service. SMP will endeavor to provide User the opportunity to review the request with SMP if SMP believes there are specific concerns or legal obligations. SMP is not responsible for managing any deletion requests that User receives directly from Customers or for the deletion of Personal Data that User collects from Customers. User is responsible for managing those deletion requests. User agrees to cooperate and ensure that any additional data User controls is handled in compliance with DP Law.

Stripe Next-Day Settlement

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Next-Day Settlement Services.

These terms (“Next-Day Settlement Terms”) supplement the General Terms and the Stripe Financial Services Terms and govern User’s use of the Next-Day Settlement Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Next-Day Settlement Terms have the meanings given to them in the General Terms or the Stripe Financial Services Terms.

2. Next-Day Settlement Timing. 

Stripe will make Next-Day Settlement Transaction funds available to User in an eligible Stripe Account, net of Refunds, Disputes, Reversals and other amounts owed to Stripe within the Next-Day Settlement Period, unless a Next-Day Settlement Delay occurs, or Stripe exercises a right under the Agreement to withhold or delay the transfer. 

3. Next-Day Settlement Conditions. 

The Next-Day Settlement Services are only available for: (a) Next-Day Settlement Payment Methods; (b) currencies designated by Stripe in the Documentation; and (c) eligible Stripe Accounts that Stripe has enabled for use of the Next-Day Settlement Services.

4. International Transactions.

This section applies only if Stripe has enabled User to make International Transactions. If International Transactions on a given day exceed 10% of the average daily Transaction volume Stripe processes for User as measured during the trailing four-week period, Stripe may extend the Next-Day Settlement Period for all International Transactions to the default Payout Schedule for the applicable Territory and Next-Day Settlement Payment Method until the average daily Transaction volume processed for User during the trailing four-week period falls below that threshold.

5. Termination of the Next-Day Settlement Services.

User may terminate its use of the Next-Day Settlement Services in respect of an eligible Stripe Account at any time in the Stripe Dashboard. Termination will become effective on the next business day. Following termination of the Next-Day Settlement Services, settlement of Transaction funds to the Stripe Account will be made in accordance with the default Payout Speed for the applicable Payment Method.

6. Definitions.

“International Transaction” means a Transaction Stripe processes for User in which the charge is presented in a different currency to the default currency of the Territory.

“Next-Day Settlement” means the settlement of Next-Day Settlement Transaction funds to an eligible Stripe Account within one business day.

“Next-Day Settlement Delay” means a delay to a Next-Day Settlement caused by: (a) the unavailability of a Financial Provider, Governmental Authority, telecommunications provider, or internet service provider; (b) incorrect information, such as a bank account number, provided to Stripe; (c) User’s equipment, software, or other technology; (d) a Force Majeure Event; (e) fraud or sanction screening as required or requested by Stripe, its Financial Providers, or recipient banks; (f) a delay or failure of a Financial Provider to settle a Transaction to Stripe, including as a result of a Financial Provider’s default, insolvency, or bankruptcy; or (g) the recipient bank not being directly integrated with the clearing system used by Stripe or the type of recipient bank account not being supported by Stripe (e.g., a savings account, money market or investment account, multi-currency account, or foreign or correspondent account).

“Next-Day Settlement Payment Method” means a Payment Method that Stripe has enabled for User’s use of the Next-Day Settlement Services.

“Next-Day Settlement Period” means the period of one business day from the date of the successful capture of the Next-Day Settlement Transaction.

“Next-Day Settlement Services” means the Services that enable User to make a Next-Day Settlement.

“Next-Day Settlement Transaction” means a Next-Day Settlement Payment Method transaction request initiated via the Stripe Technology through which Stripe is directed to capture funds for or from a payer’s associated account with respect to a payment from a Customer to User, and includes the authorization, settlement, and if applicable, Disputes, Refunds, and Reversals with respect to that Next-Day Settlement Payment Method transaction request.

“Territory” means the location of User’s Stripe Account(s).

Stripe Orchestrated Services

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms.

These terms (“Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms”) supplement the General Terms which are incorporated into these terms by this reference, and govern the use of the Stripe Orchestrated Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms.

2. User's Obligations; Services Restrictions.

2.1 User Obligations.

(a) Ensuring Third-Party PSP Compliance. User must:

(i) provide Stripe with evidence of each Third-Party PSP’s compliance with PCI-DSS upon Stripe’s request, on an annual basis;

(ii) list each Third-Party PSP as User's third-party service provider in User's PCI attestation of compliance;

(iii) immediately notify Stripe if User becomes aware that a Third-Party PSP is not complying with PCI-DSS or is otherwise failing to safeguard the Orchestrated Data;

(iv) upon request, provide Stripe with information about a Third-Party PSP that Stripe determines is necessary to enable Stripe to comply with Law or otherwise fulfill its obligations to User, User's Customers, Stripe’s users, and Stripe’s Financial Providers;

(v) upon request, validate each Third-Party PSP’s compliance with AML and Sanctions Law on an annual basis;

(vi) upon request and to the extent permitted by Law, User must provide Stripe with information about transactions with Third-Party PSPs using the Orchestrated Services that Stripe determines is necessary to enable Stripe to comply with any investigations, administrative inquiries, legal requirements, audits, and demands or inquiries from consumers, merchants, or Third-Party PSPs;

(vii) immediately disable any User-hosted Third-Party PSP integration if Stripe, in its sole discretion, notifies User that the Third-Party PSP is a Restricted Third-Party PSP;

(viii) not use the Stripe Orchestrated Services with any Third Party PSP that does not comply with Law (including AML and Sanctions Law) and data security standards imposed by Law or Financial Providers (including PCI-DSS), or are otherwise listed as prohibited in the Documentation.

(b) Custom Payment Methods. User must:

(i) ensure that User’s Customers are presented with the Custom Payment Method they select; and

(ii) immediately remove any Custom Payment Method that Stripe notifies User is prohibited.

(c) Data. User must:

(i) ensure that Orchestrated Input Data is accurate, complete, and current;

(ii) promptly provide Stripe with Orchestrated Input Data after a payment is processed where required in the Documentation;

(iii) not use the Orchestrated Output Data to develop, test, validate, train, enhance, or deploy any machine learning models or algorithms that are a substitute for, or substantially similar to, the Services;

(iv) not modify, create derivative works from, disassemble, decompile, or otherwise reverse engineer or attempt to derive any source code or underlying structure, ideas, or algorithms from the Orchestrated Output Data; and

(v) not use the Orchestrated Output Data for marketing purposes.

(d) General Obligations.

(i) User (or if applicable, User’s Affiliate) must maintain a Third-Party-PSP Agreement with each Third Party PSP.

(ii) User must obtain and maintain all necessary rights, consents, approvals, and authorizations, and provide all disclosures to Customers, as required to enable Stripe and its Affiliates to lawfully: (i) provide the Stripe Orchestrated Services; (ii) store and use User's Security Credentials and any related data, and any Orchestrated Input Data as described in the Agreement and in the Documentation; and (iii) collect, use, retain and disclose the Orchestrated Data Stripe sends or receives in connection with the Stripe Orchestrated Services, as the Agreement, Stripe’s Privacy Policy and the Documentation describe.

(iii) User must only use the Orchestrated Services in accordance with all applicable: (i) agreements with and obligations owed to relevant third parties (including Third Party PSPs); (ii) guidelines, bylaws, rules and regulations maintained by the Payment Method providers (including Card Network Rules); and (iii) data security standards imposed by Law, Financial Providers, and Payment Method Rules (including PCI-DSS and EMVCo 3DS specifications, as applicable).

(iv) If User is using the Stripe Orchestrated Services in connection with Orchestrated Transactions where the merchant is a User Affiliate or a Sub-user of a User Affiliate, then User must: (i) upon request, promptly notify Stripe of the identity of that merchant; (ii) ensure that merchant is subject to and will comply with the terms of the Agreement as if references to “User” were references to that merchant; and (iii) have sufficient controls in place to ensure that none of those Orchestrated Transactions (A) involve goods or services prohibited under AML and Sanctions Law, and (B) are for the benefit of Sanctioned Persons.

2.2 Services Restrictions.

User must not:

(a) integrate with Restricted Third-Party PSPs or Restricted Custom Payment Methods;

(b) represent that Stripe acts as the payment processor in connection with any transactions for which Stripe provides Stripe Orchestration Services rather than Stripe Payment Services; or

(c) misrepresent to anyone that Stripe is the payment processor of the Orchestrated Transactions.

3. Stripe’s Rights.

Stripe may, at any time, add or remove Restricted Custom Payment Methods or Restricted Third-Party PSPs.

4. Data Use.

4.1 Stripe’s Processing.

Stripe will process Orchestrated Data in accordance with the Agreement. As between the parties, Stripe owns all IP Rights in and to the Orchestrated Output Data.

4.2 Transfer and Custody of Orchestrated Output Data.

User authorizes Stripe to transfer Orchestrated Output Data to User or to the Third-Party PSP User designates via the Stripe Orchestrated Services. Upon Stripe’s request, User will assist Stripe in complying with its obligations under Law, which assistance may include responding to requests by data subjects or a Governmental Authority.

5. Marks requirements.

If User uses any trademark, name, logo, icon, design element, or anything else used to identify a payment method (a “Payment Method Mark”) in connection with the Stripe Orchestrated Services, User must (and must ensure that third parties):

(a) obtain and maintain all necessary rights to use the Payment Method Mark (including in connection with the Stripe Orchestrated Services);

(d) not use the Payment Method Mark of a payment method provider to identify a different payment method provider.

Stripe disclaims all responsibility and liability for:

(d) the accuracy of information User, any Third-Party PSP, any Governmental Authority, or other third party provides to Stripe that Stripe relies on to provide the Orchestrated Services. (g) any movement of funds related to Orchestrated Transactions;

8. Definitions.

“AML and Sanctions Law” means all applicable anti-money laundering and sanctions laws, rules, regulations and other binding requirements of any Governmental Authority with jurisdiction in the United States, or with jurisdiction over the Stripe Orchestrated Services, Stripe or its Affiliates, User or its Affiliates, or a Third Party PSP, as applicable.

“Card Network” means a payment card network, including the network operated by each of Visa, Mastercard, American Express, and Discover.

“Card Network Rules” means the Payment Method Rules published by a Card Network.

“Connected Account” means (a) a Platform User that has a Stripe Account onboarded to a Stripe Connect Platform via the Stripe Connect Services; or (b) a Payout Recipient.

“Custom Payment Method” means a third-party payment method not supported by a User’s Stripe integration configured to process transactions outside of Stripe that Stripe permits User to integrate with User’s use of the Stripe Orchestrated Services, as described in the Documentation.

“Orchestrated Data” means data that User sends to or receives from Stripe using the Stripe Orchestrated Services, which include response data that Stripe receives from Third-Party PSPs on User’s behalf, and may include Orchestrated Output Data, Orchestrated Input Data, and Personal Data.

“Orchestrated Input Data” means any data provided by User to Stripe to enable Stripe to perform the Orchestrated Services, including (i) any third party data included in a Standalone 3DS request as described in the Documentation or otherwise envisaged by the Stripe Orchestrated Services, including without limitation acquirer BIN, merchant ID, and requestor ID and (ii) the results of previously-provided Orchestrated Input Data, which may include accuracy rate, outcome of the payment, User’s decision to process or decline the transaction, updates to the payment status, dispute rate, and dispute code, among other things.

“Orchestrated Output Data” means any data User receives through the Stripe Orchestrated Services which has been produced by a proprietary Stripe model.

“PCI-DSS” means the Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard.

“Restricted Custom Payment Method” means a third-party payment method User is prohibited from accepting in connection with User’s use of the Stripe Orchestrated Services, as described in the Documentation and as may be updated by Stripe from time to time.

“Restricted Third-Party PSP” means a Third-Party PSP with which User may not integrate in relation to User’s use of the Stripe Orchestrated Services, as described in the Documentation and as may be updated by Stripe from time to time.

“Sanctioned Persons” means people or entities that are subject to sanctions (e.g., prohibitions or asset freezes) under AML and Sanctions Law, including if they are (a) on an applicable sanctions list, such as the sanctions lists identified by the United States Office of Foreign Asset Control and the European Commission; (b) owned or controlled by a person on an applicable sanctions list; or (c) ordinarily resident in a jurisdiction identified as high risk in the Stripe Restricted Business List (located on the Stripe website).

“Security Credentials” means User's (or if applicable, User's Affiliate’s) API license keys and other security credentials for Third-Party PSPs.

“Stripe Losses” means all amounts awarded to the third party making a Claim, and all penalties, fines, and third-party costs (including legal fees) paid by the Stripe Parties.

“Stripe Orchestrated Services” means the Services that enable User to (a) receive Output Data from Stripe for specified purposes; (b) send certain Orchestrated Data to Third-Party PSPs; (c) capture and store Orchestrated Data from Third-Party PSPs; (d) send the User Input Data to Stripe; and (e) otherwise conduct certain activities related to User’s business and transactions processed through Custom Payment Methods or Third-Party PSPs.

“Sub-users” means the users of User's or User's Affiliate’s platform services that the applicable user entity has enabled to accept card payments.

“Third-Party PSP” means User or User's Affiliate’s third-party payment services provider (such as a provider of payments processing, payments orchestration services, or token management services) to whom User directly or indirectly routes Orchestrated Data.

“Third-Party-PSP Agreement” means a service agreement between User (or if applicable, a User’s Affiliate) and a Third-Party PSP governing the routing, processing, settlement, or other ancillary services, for payment card transactions, which are being accessed in connection with the Stripe Orchestrated Services.

Stripe Organizations

Last modified: November 18, 2025

1. Stripe Organizations Services.

These terms (“Stripe Organizations Terms”) supplement the General Terms and govern the use of the Stripe Organizations Services. Each Stripe Account that is an Organization Account is bound by these Stripe Organization Terms. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Organizations Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms.

2. Conditions of Use.

Each Stripe Account can only join one Organization at any time. All Stripe Accounts within an Organization must be Affiliates. If User experiences a Change of Control and User’s Organization Account is no longer an Affiliate of the other Stripe users in the Organization, then User must immediately remove its Organization Account from the Organization.

3. Stripe Rights.

Stripe may remove, at its discretion and at any time, an Organization Account from an Organization if Stripe determines that an Organization Account is not an Affiliate of the other Organization Accounts, or is in violation of the Agreement or these Stripe Organization Terms.

4. Administration of Organization.

By joining an Organization, User authorizes (a) Stripe to share User’s account data with other Organization Accounts in User’s Organization; (b) those individuals with authority to act on behalf of individual Organization Accounts, to do so through the Organization; and (c) all other legal entities of the Organization Accounts that are part of the same Organization to enable paid features or other paid Services for the Organization, to add or remove other Affiliate Stripe Accounts in the Organization, and to access, share, delete and modify the account data of User’s Organization Account based on administration roles that User sets for that Stripe Account.

5. Sharing of Data.

User must have provided all notices and obtained all rights and consents from applicable individuals, including from all Customers, and maintain such rights and consents throughout the Term, necessary to permit Stripe to lawfully disclose Personal Data to other Organization Accounts in User’s Organization. User must not enable or allow any third party to access or use (a) User’s Organization Account, or (b) any data including Personal Data associated with any Organization Account in User’s Organization, in a manner that violates Law, including Law applicable to User or to other Organization Accounts in User’s Organization.

6. Liability of Organization Accounts.

While User’s Stripe Account is part of an Organization, User is jointly and severally liable with the other legal entities whose Stripe Accounts are part of the same Organization as User’s Organization Accounts for all activity related to use of Stripe Organizations Services, including for any features or other Services used by the Organization.

7. Customer and Payment Method Sharing (Preview).

7.1 Customer and Payment Method Sharing.

This section applies only if User uses the Customer and Payment Method Sharing feature of Stripe Organizations Services.

7.2 Permitted Use.

If User enables Customer and Payment Method Sharing for User’s Organization, User’s CPM Information can be shared with the other Organization Accounts in User’s Organization.

7.3 Conditions of Use.

Before enabling Customer and Payment Method Sharing, User must provide all necessary notices and obtain all necessary rights and consents from applicable individuals, as required by Law, before sharing CPM Information with other Organization Accounts in User’s Organization.

User agrees that:

(a) if User enables sharing of User’s CPM Information with User’s Organization, then all of User’s CPM Information is shared with other Organization Accounts that have also enabled sharing CPM Information. CPM Information cannot be segregated and shared in part; and

(b) once enabled, Customer and Payment Method Sharing can only be disabled by Stripe.

If Customer and Payment Method Sharing is disabled, User may lose access to all CPM Information.

7.4 Stripe’s Rights.

Customer and Payment Method Sharing is only available for Organizations approved by Stripe. Stripe reserves the right to disable Customer and Payment Method Sharing for any Organization or for any Organization Account in an Organization. Stripe disclaims all liability for disabling Customer and Payment Method Sharing after User has enabled or used the feature. Stripe reserves the right to apply or change fees for the Customer and Payment Method Sharing feature, subject to any notice period required by Law before applying or changing fees, as well as any applicable Taxes. Stripe reserves the right to discontinue offering the Customer and Payment Method Sharing feature at any time.

8. Definitions.

“Customer and Payment Method Information” (or “CPM Information”) means Customer account information including Customer name, Customer contact information, Customer billing information, Customer tax ID, Customer loyalty program information, and Customer’s payment methods saved to the Customer profile.

“Customer and Payment Method Sharing” means the feature that enables User to share Customer Payment Method Information of User’s Organization Accounts with other Organization Accounts in User’s Organization.

“Organization” means a group of two or more of User’s or User’s Affiliates’ Stripe Accounts that User joins together via the Stripe Organization Services.

“Organization Account” means a Stripe Account that is included in an Organization.

“Stripe Organizations Services” means the Service that is designed to enable User to join one or more Affiliate Stripe Accounts together in an Organization.

Stripe Radar

Last modified: September 28, 2026

1. Stripe Radar Services.

These terms (“Stripe Radar Terms”) supplement the General Terms and, together with the Stripe Payments Terms (as applicable), and the Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms (as applicable to use of the Stripe Radar API Service), which are incorporated into these Stripe Radar Terms by this reference govern the use of the Stripe Radar Services and Stripe Radar Data. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Radar Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms, Stripe Payments Terms, and the Stripe Orchestrated Services Terms.

2. Stripe Radar Services on by Default.

The Stripe Radar Services may be enabled by default. If User does not wish to use these Services, User must contact Stripe to disable them.

3. Permitted Use.

User may use the Stripe Radar Services and Stripe Radar Data only:

(a) to help detect and block actual or potential fraud and unauthorized Transactions, in each case in connection with the Services; and

(b) in the case of the Stripe Radar API Service only, to help detect and block actual or potential fraud and unauthorized transactions in each case related to User’s transactions processed through Third-Party PSPs.

User may use the Radar for Platforms Service to help detect and investigate whether Connected Accounts may be engaging in actual or potential fraudulent behavior, in each case in connection with the Services.

4. Services Restrictions.

User must not, and must not enable or allow any third party to:

(a) use the Stripe Radar Services or Stripe Radar Data as a factor in determining a person’s eligibility for credit, insurance, housing or employment, or in any way that could cause Stripe to be a “consumer reporting agency” or cause the Stripe Radar Data to constitute a “consumer report,” in each case as described in the FCRA (as applicable);

(b) sell, rent, transfer, make available, or communicate orally or through other means Stripe Radar Data (including as the term “sell” is defined in the CCPA, as applicable);

(c) disclose (except as Law requires or this Agreement otherwise permits), download or store Stripe Radar Data;

(d) use the Stripe Radar Services or Stripe Radar Data to: (i) develop, test, validate, train, enhance or deploy any machine learning models or algorithms that are a substitute for, or substantially similar to, the Services; or (ii) otherwise directly or indirectly develop or otherwise make available on a commercial basis any security or fraud detection product or service;

(e) use the Stripe Radar Services or Stripe Radar Data to discriminate based on race, gender, or other protected characteristics, or take any “adverse action” as that term is described in the FCRA (as applicable);

(f) use the Stripe Radar Services or Stripe Radar Data for marketing purposes;

(g) use the Stripe Radar for Platforms Service: (i) for any purpose not associated with a live Transaction (e.g. test or dummy Account Features); or (ii) as the sole input into any decision making process (e.g. automated decision making, profiling) User applies when deciding to begin engaging, cease engaging, or refrain from engaging in a business relationship with any Connected Accounts; or

(h) except as Section 3 of the Stripe Radar Terms permits, use the Stripe Radar Services or Stripe Radar Data to evaluate transactions not processed through the Services.

5. Responsibility for Decisions and Disclaimers.

5.1 Responsibility for Decisions.

(a) Use of the Stripe Radar Services and Stripe Radar Data. User is solely responsible for User’s use of the Stripe Radar Services and Stripe Radar Data, as well as the decisions User makes and the actions User takes in connection with the Stripe Radar Services and Stripe Radar Data.

(b) Stripe Radar Services Rules. User is solely responsible for choosing which rules (default or custom) User uses with the Stripe Radar Services. User’s choice to input and use additional rules with the Stripe Radar Services may result in longer settlement times for applicable Transactions. Without further notice to User, Stripe may make general or specific changes to the rules that may alter the Stripe Radar Services.

(c) Connected Accounts. User will provide Connected Accounts with the opportunity to seek human review of any decisions User makes where the Stripe Radar for Platforms Service was used as an input into that decision. If a Connected Account provides User with any additional information that indicates what User believes to be imprecision, inaccuracies, or errors in the Stripe Radar for Platforms Service and/or in the operation of the Stripe Radar for Platforms Service, User will provide Stripe with this additional information in a manner prescribed in the Documentation.

5.2 Disclaimers.

(a) Radar Scores. Each Radar Score is based on data available to the Stripe Radar Services at the time it is generated, and the Radar Score does not auto-update to reflect a subsequent change to data that was used to generate that Radar Score.

(b) Data from Transactions. The Stripe Radar Services use data from transactions processed through the Stripe services, which may include User Transactions.

(c) Nature of Stripe Radar Data. The Stripe Radar Data does not constitute legal or compliance advice, or advice as to whether User should proceed with a transaction with another person.

(d) Stripe Liability. Stripe is not liable for any losses, damages, costs, fines, fees, or penalties relating to (i) fraudulent Transactions and other fraudulent activity, including fraudulent activity untaken by third party payment service providers; (ii) non-fraudulent transactions that may be blocked by the Stripe Radar Services; or (iii) the accuracy (or inaccuracy) of the Stripe Radar Data, and any actions that User or Stripe may take based on the Stripe Radar Data.

6. Additional Terms Regarding Personal Data and Compliance.

6.1 Privacy Disclosures.

In providing the Stripe Radar Services, Stripe collects, uses and may make available to other Stripe users information regarding device characteristics and customer activity indicators that may help Stripe users detect and block actual or potential fraud. User affirms that User’s Privacy Policy fully discloses to Customers these types of data collection and use, including sharing this data with Stripe and Stripe using this data. More information is available under the topic “Advanced Fraud Protection” in the Documentation and in Stripe's Privacy Policy.

6.2 Example Disclosure.

User may choose to add the following to User’s Privacy Policy if it does not already include a disclosure to this effect:

“We use Stripe for payment, analytics, and other business services. Stripe collects transaction and personally identifying information, which it analyzes and uses to operate and improve the services it provides to us, including for fraud detection. User can learn more about Stripe and read its privacy policy here.”

6.3 Requests for Compliance Information.

Within 14 days after Stripe’s request, User must provide information and documentation that Stripe requests for the purpose of ensuring User’s compliance with these Stripe Radar Terms. Stripe may suspend or terminate User’s access to the Stripe Radar Services immediately upon notice to User if User fails to provide information or documentation Stripe requests under this Section 6.3.

7. Stripe Radar Data.

Stripe Radar Data is Stripe Data for the purposes of this Agreement. User must promptly delete or destroy all Stripe Radar Data in User’s possession or control that User received in connection with the Stripe Radar Services (a) upon termination of these Stripe Radar Terms; and (b) at any time upon Stripe’s request. User may retain copies of Stripe Radar Data, if required by Law.

8. Definitions.

“Account Features” means Stripe’s merchant level fraud protection system.

“CCPA” means California Consumer Privacy Act of 2018, Cal. Civ. Code Sections 1798.100-1798.199.

“Connected Account” means (a) a Platform User that has a Stripe Account onboarded to a Stripe Connect Platform via the Stripe Connect Services; or (b) a Payout Recipient.

“FCRA” means Fair Credit Reporting Act, 15 U.S.C. Section 1681, et seq. and Equal Credit Opportunity Act, 15 U.S.C. Section 1681, et seq.

“Radar Score” means a numerical risk score or level associated with a transaction, Connected Account, or other related activity that the Stripe Radar Services provides.

“Stripe Radar API Service” means the Services, including an API, that (a) provide User with a means of sending to Stripe the Orchestrated Input Data and access to the Stripe Radar Services and the Orchestrated Output Data, and (b) is designed to enable User to help detect and prevent actual or potential fraud, or unauthorized transactions, related to User’s business and transactions processed through other payment service providers.

“Stripe Radar Data” means the Radar Scores and other data User receives through the Stripe Radar Services, and the Stripe Orchestrated Services (as applicable).

“Stripe Radar for Platforms Service” means the Services that are designed to enable User to help detect and investigate whether Connected Accounts may be engaging in actual or potential fraudulent behavior, in each case in connection with the Services.

“Stripe Radar Services” means the Services that are designed to enable User to detect and evaluate the risk that a transaction, Connected Account, or other related activity is fraudulent.

2. Stablecoin Treasury Services.

2.1 Overview; Stripe's Rights and Obligations.

The Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services enable User to hold funds with the Stablecoin Partner, and access and use the Stablecoin Partner Services. Stripe does not convert, hold custody of, or transfer any Stablecoin as a component of the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services. Stripe may add or restrict access in User's Stripe Account to particular denominations of Stablecoin or fiat currency, or types of Stablecoin Partner Transactions, at any time. Because the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services necessarily involve and require the performance of Stablecoin Partner Services, User's use of the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services constitutes User's simultaneous access to and use of the Stablecoin Partner Services.

2.2 Stablecoin Partner.

The Stablecoin Partner is a Financial Provider, and the Stablecoin Partner Terms, which govern the Stablecoin Partner's rights and obligations with respect to performing User's Stablecoin Partner Transactions, are Financial Provider Terms. By using the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services, User agrees to be bound by the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms in addition to these Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Terms. Stripe will identify the applicable Stablecoin Partner to User during the onboarding process for the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services, and Stripe may add or remove Stablecoin Partners in its sole discretion. To access and use the Stablecoin Partner Services, User must (a) be located and incorporated in a jurisdiction that Stripe and the Stablecoin Partner support in connection with the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services; (b) accept the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms; and (c) comply with the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms at all times. The Stablecoin Partner may add or remove supported Stablecoins and fiat currencies as provided in its Stablecoin Partner Terms, at any time.

2.3 Disclaimer.

To the extent Law permits, Stripe is not liable to User for any losses, damages, or costs resulting from: (a) the suspension or termination of, or interruptions to, the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services or the Stablecoin Partner Services; (b) User providing incorrect information in connection with a Stablecoin Partner Transaction; (c) User or any third party attempting to initiate a Stablecoin Partner Transaction that is not supported by the Stablecoin Partner; (d) the Stablecoin Partner rejecting a Stablecoin Partner Transaction, for any reason; (e) the Stablecoin Partner rejecting, delaying, or failing to complete a Stablecoin Payout Transaction for any reason; (f) the Stablecoin Partner’s default, insolvency, or bankruptcy; or (g) the irreversibility of any Stablecoin Partner Transaction, including any exchange of fiat currency for Stablecoin that cannot be unwound once initiated by the Stablecoin Partner.

2.4 Depeg Event.

If a Depeg Event occurs, Stripe and the Stablecoin Partner may immediately (a) suspend the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services with respect to the affected Stablecoin, including suspending any new exchanges of fiat currency for the affected Stablecoin; or (b) continue providing the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services with respect to the affected Stablecoin at its prevailing Stablecoin-to-fiat market exchange rate. The value of Stablecoin held in custody with the Stablecoin Partner during a Depeg Event is governed solely by the Stablecoin Partner Terms. Stripe is not liable to User for any losses, damages, or costs arising from a Depeg Event, or any actions taken by Stripe or the Stablecoin Partner in connection with a Depeg Event, including any difference between the exchange rate applicable at the time User accepts payment in Stablecoin and the rate at which the Stablecoin Partner executes the exchange, if it chooses to do so.

3. Taxes.

Transactions in Stablecoin may be subject to Taxes. User acknowledges that tax Laws applicable to Stablecoin transactions may be uncertain and evolving, and that it is not Stripe's responsibility to assess, collect, report, or remit any Taxes owed by User arising from: (a) the use of the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services; (b) the use of the Stablecoin Partner Services; or (c) any other transactions User may enter into with a Stablecoin Partner. Stripe does not provide investment or tax advice. Stripe may be required to submit tax information reports to tax authorities regarding these transactions and may send User a copy of such tax information. Specifically, Law may require Stripe to file periodic informational returns with taxing authorities related to User's use of the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services, and Stripe may send tax-related information electronically to User. The Stablecoin Partner may also have independent tax reporting obligations with respect to Stablecoin Partner Transactions, as described in the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms.

4. Privacy.

User authorizes Stripe and its Affiliates to share information about User, including Personal Data, with the Stablecoin Partner as necessary to enable User to access and use the Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services and the Stablecoin Partner Services. User must ensure that, where required by Law, User's customers and counterparties are informed that User uses Stripe and the Stablecoin Partner in connection with Stablecoin-related services, and that their data may be processed by the Stablecoin Partner in accordance with the Stablecoin Partner's privacy policy, as made available in the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms.

5. Definitions.

“Depeg Event” means any occurrence where Stripe or the Stablecoin Partner determines, in its sole discretion, that the price of a Stablecoin has failed to maintain its intended peg to the fiat value to which it is linked.

"Stablecoin" means any fiat-backed cryptocurrency supported by a Stablecoin Partner, which may be updated from time to time as stated in the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms and the Documentation.

"Stripe Stablecoin Treasury Services" means the user interface, data transmission, user support, and related technology services that enable User to access and use the Stablecoin Partner Services.

"Stablecoin Partner" means an entity, which may be a Stripe Affiliate or a third party, that provides Stablecoin-related services to User, including the exchange, custody, transmission, or payout of Stablecoin, under the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms.

"Stablecoin Partner Services" means any services provided by a Stablecoin Partner to User in connection with Stripe's Stablecoin-related Services, which may include: (a) the creation and maintenance of accounts and associated wallet addresses for User; (b) the exchange or conversion of Stablecoins to fiat currency and vice versa; (c) the custody of Stablecoins; (d) the transmission or payout of Stablecoins to designated wallet addresses; and (e) such other Stablecoin-related services as the Stablecoin Partner may offer from time to time, as described in the applicable Stablecoin Partner Terms.

"Stablecoin Partner Terms" means the terms that govern the Stablecoin Partner's provision, and User's access to and use of, the Stablecoin Partner Services, and which may be updated from time to time.

“Stablecoin Partner Transaction” means a Stablecoin or fiat currency transmission, exchange, custody, or other transaction that is requested or executed via the Stablecoin Partner Services.

6. Regional Terms.

1. Stripe Tax Services.

These terms (“Stripe Tax Terms”) supplement the General Terms and govern the use of the Stripe Tax Services. Capitalized terms that are not defined in these Stripe Tax Terms have the same meanings given to them in the General Terms.

2. Provision of Services.

2.1 Partner Services. 

Some of the Stripe Tax Services are provided directly by Stripe and some are Partner Services. Stripe provides User access to Partner Services for User’s convenience only and Stripe does not approve or endorse Partner Services. User’s access and use of Partner Services is at User’s own risk and Stripe disclaims all responsibility and liability for User’s use of Partner Services. Partner Services are not Services. User may be required to create an account with a Tax Partner and agree to its terms and privacy policy for use of the Partner Services. Stripe fees apply to Partner Services that User accesses via the Services. User’s obligation to pay fees and Stripe’s rights to collect fees under this Agreement apply to Partner Services. When using the Partner Services, User must respond in a timely manner to the Tax Partner’s requests for additional information or documentation in connection with the Partner Services. 

2.2 Use of TaxJar. 

For purposes of these Stripe Tax Terms, TPS Unlimited, Inc, dba TaxJar (“TaxJar”), is deemed a Tax Partner and may provide Partner Services. User’s use of TaxJar services in connection with Stripe Tax Services constitutes User’s acceptance of the TaxJar Terms of Service and acknowledgement of the TaxJar Privacy Policy.

2.3 Subcontractors.

(a) Subcontractors of Stripe Tax Services. When a subcontractor is providing the Stripe Tax Services on behalf of Stripe, in certain countries, the subcontractor may be required by Law to also provide remittance services. For those additional services, the subcontractor’s terms and fees will apply, and Stripe is not provisioning any such additional services. 

(b) GVC. When Global VAT Compliance B.V. (“GVC”) is acting as a subcontractor of Stripe Tax Services, GVC may process Personal Data as an independent Data Controller in order to comply with GVC’s obligations under Law, in accordance with GVC’s Privacy Policy.

2.4 Agent; Power of Attorney. 

For Stripe Tax Services that are not Partner Services, User (i) grants Stripe (and if applicable, Stripe’s subcontractors) a limited power of attorney and appoints Stripe (and if applicable, Stripe’s subcontractors) as its attorney-in-fact and agent, with the full power and authority to perform all required activities as User could otherwise do itself or in person; and (ii) authorizes Stripe (and if applicable, Stripe’s subcontractors) to access User’s tax account with a Governmental Authority using its Tax Information and act on User’s behalf.

3. User’s Obligations.

3.1 Information and Documentation. 

(a) Accuracy of Tax Information. User acknowledges that the Stripe Tax Services rely on the accuracy and completeness of information, including Tax Information, User provides to Stripe, and User is solely responsible for the accuracy and completeness of all information. Inaccurate or incomplete information may (i) result in Stripe not being able to provide some or all of the Stripe Tax Services; (ii) cause the Stripe Tax Services to generate inaccurate Stripe Tax Data; or (iii) cause inaccurate or incomplete tax calculations, Tax Registrations, or Tax Filings.

(b) Additional Information Required. User must (i) promptly provide Stripe, within the timeframes that Stripe specifies, all information and documentation that Stripe reasonably requests in connection with the Stripe Tax Services; and (ii) sign, and legalize (where required), any additional documents Stripe requires to provide the Stripe Tax Services, including any additional powers of attorney or authorizations required by a Governmental Authority for Stripe to provide the Stripe Tax Services.

3.2 Authorization. 

User must ensure that User’s Tax Representative is authorized to provide the Tax Information, submit a Tax Registration, and approve Tax Filings on User’s behalf at all times during the Term. At Stripe’s request, User must provide additional information or documentation demonstrating User’s Tax Representative’s authority.

3.3 Filing and Registration. 

User must:

(a) review all draft Tax Filings or information that will be used to prepare and file a Tax Filing for accuracy and completeness and promptly notify Stripe of any corrections or additional information within the timeframe Stripe specifies;

(b) pay all associated fees, costs, and penalties that the relevant Governmental Authorities require for each Tax Registration and Tax Filing; and

(c) timely remit the payment of any other taxes or fees associated with each Tax Filing.

Stripe will not independently audit or verify the Tax Information or any draft Tax Filing. After the earlier of (i) User’s approval; or (ii) an approval deadline being passed, the draft Tax Filing will be deemed approved and Stripe will submit the Tax Filing on User's behalf.

3.4 User’s Tax Policies. 

User is solely responsible for its own tax policies and tax reporting positions.

4. Stripe Tax Data.

4.1 Limitations on Reporting. 

User may receive Stripe Tax Data through the Stripe Tax Services that is based on Stripe’s interpretations of Law. User must validate that Stripe’s interpretations are appropriate for its circumstances.

4.2 Stripe Tax Data. 

Stripe Tax Data is Stripe Data for purposes of this Agreement. User is responsible for retaining copies of Stripe Tax Data for the time period required by Law.

5. Service Restrictions. 

5.1 Fiscal Representation. 

Stripe does not provide fiscal representation. Where Law or a Governmental Authority requires fiscal representation in connection with the Stripe Tax Services, a subcontractor engaged by Stripe may act as fiscal representative on User's behalf. User may be required to execute additional documents in connection with any such fiscal representation arrangement, including any authorizations or agreements required by the relevant Governmental Authority.

5.2 Protected Health Information. 

User must not, and must ensure that its Connected Accounts (if applicable) do not, use the Stripe Tax Services in connection with Protected Health Information.

6. Disclaimer. 

6.1 No Tax Advice or Audit Support. 

User acknowledges that Stripe does not provide legal, tax or accounting advice, and that the Stripe Tax Services do not include audit support. The Stripe Tax Services are automated software tools provided strictly for User’s convenience. User is solely responsible for conducting its own due diligence and seeking the assistance of qualified legal, tax, and accounting professionals. All information, forms, and materials provided in connection with the Stripe Tax Services are provided to User for informational and educational purposes only. 

6.2 Third Party Data Sources. 

User acknowledges that Stripe Tax Services rely on integrations with third party data sources and governmental databases, which may be subject to latency, downtime, or inaccuracies. Stripe disclaims all liability with respect to continuous availability, latency maximums, or real-time accuracy of third-party data feeds used to generate Stripe Tax Data.

6.3. Additional Disclaimers. 

User remains fully responsible for, and Stripe Parties disclaim all liability with respect to:

(a) User’s fulfillment of its obligations under Law, including with respect to Taxes;

(b) the accuracy of the Stripe Tax Services, and User’s obligation to pay any fine, penalty, back-taxes, or other sanction imposed by a Governmental Authority as a result of the use of the Stripe Tax Services; 

(c) User’s action, or failure to act, as a result of any directions or recommendations User receives related to the Stripe Tax Services; and 

(d) additional tax or legal obligations that the Stripe Tax Services may trigger, such as requirements to register for income tax, franchise tax, or other local, state, federal, or international tax obligations.

7. Definitions.

“Connected Account” means (a) a Platform User that has a Stripe Account onboarded to a Stripe Connect Platform via the Stripe Connect Services; or (b) a Payout Recipient.

“Partner Services” means a product or service provided by a Tax Partner which Stripe enables User to access via the Services (excluding any subcontractor services) and for which Stripe and the third party service provider have agreed that Stripe fees apply.

“Stripe Tax Data” means data and reporting User receives through the Stripe Tax Services.

“Stripe Tax Services” means the Services and Partner Services relating to indirect taxes that Stripe makes available to User, including Tax Registration Services, Tax Validation Services, Tax Calculation Services, and Tax Filing Services.

“Tax Calculation Services” means the Services that enable User to determine and calculate the amount, if any, of certain Taxes due in connection with User’s sale of goods or provision of services to Customers.

“Tax Filing” means a tax return that User requests to be prepared and submitted to the appropriate Governmental Authority, arising from the sale of goods or provision of services to Customers.

“Tax Filing Services” means the Services or Partner Services that enable User to complete a Tax Filing.

“TaxJar Privacy Policy” means TaxJar’s privacy policy located at https://www.taxjar.com/privacy.

This Section 6 applies if User uses Dynamic Currency Conversion. User acknowledges that when Stripe makes Dynamic Currency Conversion available to User, Stripe is providing a technical service only to User to enable User to offer Dynamic Currency Conversion to its Customers, and Stripe is not offering or providing any service to Customers. 6.2 Services Restrictions.

(a) use Dynamic Currency Conversion except in conjunction with the Stripe Terminal Services;

(b) offer conversion optionality to its Customers except in connection with its acceptance of payments made in exchange for goods or services in connection with its business; or

(c) represent, either explicitly or implicitly, any point during the Dynamic Currency Conversion Transaction process that their use of Dynamic Currency Conversion is a service provided by the relevant Card Network.

6.3 Currencies and Exchange Rates.

The currency exchange rates made available to User as part of Dynamic Currency Conversion may differ from the currency exchange rates Stripe obtains from its service providers or publicly available resources. Stripe reserves the right to add or remove Dynamic Currency Conversion supported currencies at any time without notice. With respect to any individual Customer transaction, Stripe may choose to apply a different exchange rate than the Currency Exchange Rate when Stripe settles the resulting funds to User’s Stripe Account, or when issuing Refunds. User acknowledges and agrees that Dynamic Currency Conversion does not provide: (a) the supply of foreign currencies by Stripe; or (b) a foreign currency exchange or similar service.

6.4 User Obligations.

User must:

(a) provide Customers with all necessary information relating to its use of Dynamic Currency Conversion as required by Law or Payment Method Rules, in the form (including durable medium, as applicable) and manner required, including that this service may include a User-imposed markup on the prevailing currency exchange rate; and

(b) ensure Customers are offered a free choice to accept or decline the conversion option and that Dynamic Currency Conversion is not used to convert a Transaction to a Customer’s card currency without first obtaining the Customer’s agreement on the basis of the information duly provided;

6.5 Disclaimer.

User remains fully responsible for, and Stripe disclaims all liability with respect to:

(a) User’s fulfilment of its obligations under Law and Payment Method Rules, including with respect to its use of Dynamic Currency Conversion;

(b) the accuracy of the prices Dynamic Currency Conversion presents to User and its Customers, and the currency exchange rates Dynamic Currency Conversion uses and applies;

(c) any fees charged to User or its Customers by User’s bank or any other applicable financial institution; and

(d) User’s action, or failure to act, as a result of any directions or recommendations User receives related to Dynamic Currency Conversion.

7. Definitions.

This Section 2.4 applies if User manages or controls its Connected Accounts’ access to or use of the Stripe Terminal Software in connection with the Platform Services. Stripe, or the licensor of the Stripe Terminal Software, may from time to time make available Updates to the Stripe Terminal Software. An Update is mandatory unless Stripe designates it as optional or discretionary. Stripe will notify User of each mandatory Update that it does not push directly to User’s Connected Accounts. Upon receipt of an Update notice from Stripe, User must immediately notify User’s Connected Accounts that use the Stripe Terminal Services (a) of the Update; (b) whether the Update is mandatory, optional or discretionary; (c) if the Update is mandatory, the deadline for installing the Update; and (d) that they may not be able to continue using the Stripe Terminal Services if they do not install the mandatory Update by the deadline. User must ensure that User’s Connected Accounts install each mandatory Update by the date or within the time period stated in the notice; or, if there is no date or period stated in the notice, then no later than 30 days after the date of Stripe’s notice.

2.5 Stripe Terminal Documentation.

References to Documentation in this Agreement will be read as references to Stripe Terminal Documentation, with respect to the Stripe Terminal Services, Stripe Terminal Software and Stripe Terminal Products.

then in addition to all other remedies available to Stripe, Stripe may take one or more of the following actions:
